| R85 swift: a FACTORY-initialised or DESTRUCTURED public global drops the caller silently | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-swift `649ea46`. Pre-existing; NOT introduced by R79's fix** | **CARDINAL SIN, swift only, and it sits exactly where R79's own commit said it was "open by design".** `public let sharedWorker = makeWorker()` (a factory call rather than a direct constructor) and `public let (a, b) = (Worker(), 42)` (destructured) both leave the CALLER absent from `functions[]` entirely. Coordinator's repro, ground truth EXECUTED (`swift build && ./.build/debug/App` writes the file): with `public let sharedWorker = Worker()` the report carries `<main>` and `run` with `Fs`; change that ONE line to `= makeWorker()`, holding everything else constant, and the report contains only `Worker.doWork` — `run` and `<main>` are gone. Gate matrix measured across five forms: `deny Fs` 1 and `deny Fs Unknown` 1 (**caught only incidentally, because the CALLEE is still independently reported — not because the caller was judged**), but **`deny Unknown` 0** (so there is no disclosure either — a true silent gap, not a coarser answer), **`deny Fs run` 0** and **`deny Fs <main>` 0** — an ordinary "the app layer must not touch Fs directly" policy passes over provable I/O. Everything that asks WHO reaches an effect is blind: scoped policies, `path` ("no function matching"), `callers`, `gains`, `tour`. Root cause read rather than guessed: `Driver.swift` populates `publicGlobalTypesByModule` only from `globalPublic`/`globalTypes` during the per-file merge; `globalFactories` is resolved in a LATER, separate pass that writes only into `globalTypesByModule` and never into the cross-module table. The destructured case fails differently — `DeclCollector` records `globalTypes`/`globalPublic` only for the plain-identifier and constructor-call binder shapes, and the tuple-destructure binder never populates `globalPublic` at all. **Two binder shapes and two population sites, one question — the drift class from brief section G, one layer down from the vein R79 closed.** | **THE LESSON, and it is about the previous row rather than this engine: "OPEN BY DESIGN, STATED NOT HIDDEN" IS NOT A SAFE CATEGORY WHEN THE OPEN SHAPE IS SILENT.** R79's commit named these three shapes as known limits and I recorded them as such, which reads as *acceptable*. Measured, two of the three are not limitations at all — they are the same cardinal sin one binder shape over, and the engine prints `nothing hidden — every effect sits where its name says it should` over them. **The distinction that matters is never open-vs-closed; it is DISCLOSED-vs-SILENT.** A named limitation that answers `Unknown` is a limitation. A named limitation that answers *nothing* is a sin with a footnote. Every future "open by design" line must say which of the two it is, and prove it. **Second finding, about the instrument rather than the engine:** R79's own ambiguity control, `testAmbiguousCrossModuleGlobalNameResolvesNothing`, asserts that `<main>` stays absent when two imported modules both declare `public let sharedWorker` — and the coordinator compiled that exact fixture: it is a **compile error** (`ambiguous use of 'sharedWorker'`). The control validates behaviour for a program shape that cannot exist, so it is evidence of nothing; one of the four controls R79 was accepted on could never have failed. This is the "instrument that cannot fail" class, found in a control written the same day the family hardened four other instruments for exactly that reason. **FIXED by making both shapes feed the SAME authority the direct-constructor shape always used**, not by adding a third write site — `DeclCollector.inferGlobalType` is now one function shared by the identifier binder and each positionally-paired tuple-destructure element, and it marks `globalPublic` on every branch including the deferred factory one (R79 had left that branch out of `globalPublic` entirely, though the public/open fact is known at parse time regardless of when the TYPE resolves). `publicGlobalTypesByModule` is no longer written at merge time; it is DERIVED once, after both passes that can populate `globalTypesByModule` have run. Controls, all measured: both sin fixtures flip ABSENT→`Fs`; a genuinely pure factory/destructured global gains nothing; a non-public one still does not resolve; R79's direct-constructor case and its remaining controls unchanged. Revert test: exactly the 2 fix-pinning tests go red, the 4 controls pass either way. 1006/1006 tests, `smoke.sh` 148/148, `fuzz.py` 25/25, `fabrication_probe.py` 28/28, `recall.sh` 5/5, `ci/self-gate.sh` OK; the two strace gates UNRUN, reported as unrun. Corpus A/B on all four projects: `functions[]` byte-identical, ADDED 0 REMOVED 0 CHANGED 0 — **SAFETY only, not recall, and measured as such: none of the four contains a single factory- or destructured-shaped public global.** **AND THE CONTROL PROBLEM WAS WORSE THAN ONE TEST.** R79's `testAmbiguousCrossModuleGlobalNameResolvesNothing` was confirmed uncompilable in BOTH language modes (`ambiguous use of 'sharedWorker'`; Swift 6 fails even earlier on a Sendable diagnostic) — **no reachable Swift program can trigger the `moduleCount > 1` branch it pinned.** Replaced with `testInternalGlobalInOneModuleDoesNotPoisonAPublicSameNameInAnother`, which COMPILES AND RUNS and exercises the real candidate-counting logic. **A SECOND vacuous control was then found in the same file:** R79's `testNonPublicCrossModuleGlobalDoesNotResolve` is also uncompilable (`cannot find 'sharedWorkerInternal' in scope`) — a direct cross-module reference to an internal symbol is definitionally invisible in Swift. Flagged, not yet fixed; it retains some value (it pins that the public/private filter excludes non-public names) but it cannot fail on the input it claims to test. **SO TWO OF THE FOUR CONTROLS R79 WAS ACCEPTED ON COULD NEVER HAVE FAILED — and the coordinator's own independent re-verification of the non-public one used an uncompilable fixture too, and reported it as a passing control.** The conclusion happened to be right; the evidence offered for it was vacuous. **A control over an input that cannot exist is not weak evidence, it is NO evidence** — no correct engine could pass it differently and no broken one would be caught — and this was written on the same day four other instruments were hardened for exactly that property. **When a control asserts an absence, compile and RUN the fixture first: prove the program exists before trusting what the engine says about it.** **RECALL EVIDENCE, obtained 2026-09-01 and previously the missing half of this vein.** Both R79's and R85's A/Bs were byte-identical over swift-nio/collections/algorithms/argument-parser, and two agents separately measured that those corpora contain ZERO top-level `public let`/`public var` — so those runs proved safety and said nothing about recall. A dedicated hunt then searched ~18 real packages plus ~19 GitHub code-search queries, grepping at column 0 to reject indented class properties (Kitura's `TodoListController.public let router` was rejected on exactly that basis). Real, unmodified top-level public globals exist in **Alamofire** (`public let AF = Session.default`), **jamf/Replicator** (`public let fm = FileManager()`), the **Kitura-Starter-Kit** template family and **quran-ios**. Two were A/B'd with the real declarations vendored verbatim: **jamf/Replicator** — a cross-module `fm.removeItem(atPath:)` read `inferred: [], unresolved: false` (proven-pure) pre-fix and `['Fs']` at HEAD, so **`deny Fs` would have exited 0 over real file deletion**; **Alamofire** — `AF.request(...)` read `['Fs']` only pre-fix, missing the real `Session.request` call entirely, and `['Fs','Net','Rand','Unknown']` at HEAD, so **`deny Net` would have exited 0 over Alamofire's own request path**. Both are single-row changes across 51 and 591 functions with zero over-charge elsewhere — the strongest form of this evidence. **And the honest negative: ZERO genuine top-level FACTORY globals were found, and exactly one destructured global (JXKit's version tuple, binding to `Int?`, no effect-bearing methods).** So R85's two shapes are real — proven by executed, revert-tested fixtures — but were NOT found in standard-layout public SwiftPM code despite that search. That bounds what this fix is worth and is recorded rather than left implied. **Two process catches worth keeping:** the hunt found `.build/release/candor-swift` STALE (mtime an hour before the R85 commit) **while `--version` printed the current string** — the standing trap, caught before it contaminated a measurement; and it self-reported a near-miss where a Kitura-based construction gave a byte-identical A/B only because the `Router` type was never vendored, so the receiver could not resolve either way — it had held the wrong thing constant, and said so instead of filing a clean bill. **CORRECTED 2026-09-02, BY MEASUREMENT: the "cannot fail" half of that claim is WRONG, and so is the review-panel finding that repeated it.** A later four-engine panel re-filed BOTH `testNonPublicFactoryInitializedGlobalDoesNotResolve` and `testNonPublicDestructuredGlobalDoesNotResolve` as vacuous on the same reasoning — the fixture does not compile, therefore the test cannot fail. **That reasoning is a non-sequitur for this engine: candor-swift is SwiftSyntax-based and NEVER BUILDS ITS INPUT**, so compilability is not what determines whether an assertion is falsifiable. The falsifiability question is whether the ENGINE's own mechanism is what produces the asserted absence, and that is measurable directly. Coordinator's A/B, one variable changed — `public` added to the two declarations, everything else byte-identical: 3a `run` **ABSENT -> `['Fs']`**; 3b `run` **ABSENT -> `['Fs']`**. The visibility filter is therefore exactly what makes the internal arm absent, so an engine with that filter removed would RESOLVE it and the tests would go RED. **Those controls have teeth.** What remains true from the original row is the narrower and still-important point about the AMBIGUITY control, which pinned a `moduleCount > 1` branch no reachable Swift program can trigger — that one really was vacuous, and it was replaced. **The lesson is not the one first drawn.** "The fixture does not compile" is a genuine red flag but it is not the test; it prompts the question, it does not answer it. The answer is a one-variable A/B showing the engine's verdict moves with the property the control claims to pin. Two separate agents and this coordinator all reasoned from compilability to vacuity without running that A/B, and all three were wrong in the same direction — over-condemning our own instruments, which is the mirror image of the failure this file mostly records. |
| R86 java: a literal argument chosen by a TERNARY is captured from whichever branch sits adjacent | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-java `1abc71d` + `2dd1600`. Pre-existing, and in published 0.34.0** | **CARDINAL SIN, java only, and the R83 question one file over — R83's `Interp.ProvInterpreter` fix never touched this surface.** Every backward-instruction-window walker in `Literals.java` (`firstLiteralArg`, `literalArgsInWindow`, `nearestLiteralArg`, `intLiteralBefore`, `reflectReceiver`, `concatArgHost`, `programHeadLiteral`) bounds its walk at a call, a jump, a `NEW` or a store — but **never at a control-flow JOIN**, a label reached by a `GOTO` from elsewhere. A ternary or switch-expression feeding a literal straight into a call argument compiles to two branches merging immediately before the call, so the walker sees only the physically adjacent branch. Coordinator's repro: `new Socket(cond ? "danger.example.net" : "safe-partner.example.com", 443)` with `net-partner safe-partner.example.com` configured — **`danger.example.net` is ABSENT from the report entirely**, and `deny Net[unknown-host]` exits **0**. **This is worse than an omission: the engine makes a POSITIVE claim about the wrong destination.** It does not say "I could not determine the host"; it says the host is the declared safe partner, and a destination-scoped policy then passes on that basis. Order-dependence confirmed by swapping which branch is dangerous — the captured host swaps with it. `deny Net` (blanket) still exits 1, since `Net` is unconditional regardless of host, so the blast radius is destination-scoped policy, `net-partner` classification, and everything downstream that reads the captured host. **The control that proves the protection mechanism exists and is specifically defeated:** the same policy over a fully-runtime host with no literal at all correctly yields `incomplete: ["Net"]` and exit 1 — so capturing NO literal fails closed, and capturing the WRONG literal fails open. | Not yet fixed. **The fix must bound the window at a join, not extend it** — a wider window would capture both literals and could as easily attribute a literal from an unrelated statement. Where two branches reach one call, the honest answer is BOTH hosts, or `incomplete`, never one of them chosen by bytecode order. Not measured, inferred to share the code path and named so the fix covers them: try/catch merges and loop-carried assignment. **Also unswept: whether other consumers of these walkers (path/tables/reflection-receiver classification) inherit the same wrong-literal claim** — the walkers are shared, so the question is not whether they do but where. **FIXED by BOUNDING the window at a join, not widening it** — `Literals.joinLabels(MethodNode)`, computed once per method, is the set of every label that is a jump/switch-case target or an exception-handler start; all eight backward walkers now stop there rather than walking through. Coordinator-verified: the ternary fixture now claims NEITHER host and emits `incomplete: ["Net"]`, and `deny Net[unknown-host]` goes exit 0 → **exit 1**. The engine stopped asserting a destination it cannot determine, which is the fail-closed direction the pre-existing runtime-host control already demonstrated. **One shared helper, so every consumer inherits it — enumerated in the fix:** Net host (bare `(String,int)` ctor, per-call window, concat-prefix, URL-terminal), Fs path, Exec program head, Db tables, reflection method-name and receiver class. The same hole was also found and closed in the single-store scanners `constStringLocals`/`constUrlLocals`, which were NOT in the list of seven I handed over — a local assigned via ternary has one `ASTORE` whose immediate predecessor differs by branch. **MY BRIEF WAS WRONG ABOUT TWO OF THE THREE INFERRED SIBLINGS, AND THE AGENT MEASURED RATHER THAN ACCEPTING IT.** Switch-expression was genuinely vulnerable in BOTH branch orders and is fixed. **Try/catch-via-local and loop-carried reassignment were already SAFE** pre-fix, via an unrelated pre-existing "two different literals stored to the same local ⇒ ambiguous" check in `constStringLocals`. I wrote those as inferred-to-share-the-path and it read as measured. **AND THE DEFECT WAS FOUND ORGANICALLY IN REAL THIRD-PARTY CODE, not only in fixtures:** `ignite-core-2.16.0` `IgniteLogInfoProviderImpl.ackClassPathWildCard` had an if/else merge before `Paths.get(...)`, and the engine was certifying the else-branch literal `"."` even when the if-branch (the common `idx > 0` case) actually ran — now correctly `incomplete: ["Fs"]`. **Isolated 325-jar A/B against a pre-image built from the exact starting commit** (separating this change from other fixes already in HEAD): **0 functions removed, 0 effect-set changes** — every difference confined to literal-detail and `incomplete` fields. 8 functions newly appear, all pure, all gaining only `incomplete: ["Db"]`, each explained via `javap`: an unrelated-branch literal was previously being absorbed into a distant SQL-literal window. Gates green; `nativeCompile`/`run_kotlin.sh` UNRUN and reported as unrun. Revert-tested: 3 of 7 new cases go red, the 4 controls hold either way. **`assert-audit.sh` flagged an unbacked assertion in the first commit and the tests in `2dd1600` are the response** — the tool doing its job on the day it shipped. |
| R87 java: an entry-point marker annotation is only seen at RUNTIME retention | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-java `1abc71d` + `2dd1600`. Pre-existing, in published 0.34.0** | **SILENT UNDER-REPORT in the query surface, java only.** `Candor.markEntryPoints` matches `Rules.ROOT_ANNOTATIONS` via `annoOrMetaMatches(mn.visibleAnnotations, …)` — **`visibleAnnotations` only**, which is ASM's RUNTIME-retention list. A custom lifecycle/scheduler/container marker declared **without an explicit `@Retention(RUNTIME)`** gets Java's DEFAULT `CLASS` retention, lands in `invisibleAnnotations`, and is never seen. That default is the single most common mistake in writing such an annotation, so the failure selects for exactly the real-world case. Measured with only the retention policy varied, everything else held constant: with `CLASS` retention `entryPoint:false` and `candor reachable` answers **`{"entryPoints":0,"effects":{}}`** — which `Query.java`'s own comment calls *"the strongest claim this tool can make — the program performs no effect at runtime"* — over a method that genuinely writes a file; with explicit `RUNTIME` it correctly reports `entryPoints:1, Fs via Job.run`. **`impact <fn>` shares the same `entryPoint()` filter and is affected identically** — and that verb is the agent edit-time feedback loop this family calls its north star. **The `--policy` scan gate is NOT affected** (a `deny <effect>` fires on a function's own direct effects regardless of entry-point status), so the blast radius is the entry-point-scoped query verbs, not the gate. | Not yet fixed, and **the authority to reuse already exists in the same file**: `anyParamAnnoMatches` (the CDI `@Observes` path) explicitly checks BOTH `visibleParameterAnnotations` and `invisibleParameterAnnotations`. The method- and class-level root-marker path never got the same treatment — two implementations of "does this element carry a root marker", one correct. Brief section G: make them share one authority rather than patching the second copy, or this recurs at the third. **FIXED by giving both paths ONE authority:** `anyDeclAnnoMatches(visible, invisible, markers)`, mirroring the `anyParamAnnoMatches` pattern that was already correct — rather than patching the second copy, which is what let the two drift in the first place. Controls measured: `CLASS` retention now `entryPoint:true`; `RUNTIME` still true; **no marker at all still false, 0 entry points** — the control that stops the fix from simply marking everything. `impact` confirmed to share the fix (0 downstream entry points pre-fix, 1 post-fix on the same fixture); `--policy` gates confirmed unaffected. **0 hits across the 325-jar corpus, and that is the EXPECTED result stated as such rather than presented as reassurance:** established libraries use correctly-retentioned framework annotations, while this defect targets bespoke markers — so the corpus cannot exercise it, and the evidence is the synthetic fixture plus its revert test, not the corpus. |
| R88 rust: the bare unannotated `let` binder never records a dispatch binding — the commonest form in the language | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-rust `ff6efad`. Pre-existing** | **CARDINAL SIN, rust only, and the Q3 "separate implementations that drift" question paying out exactly as designed.** Every sibling binder was hardened for dispatch-typed values across R71/R75/R76/R77 — if-let, while-let, match-arm, for-loop, let-else, annotated `let`, tuple destructure. **`visit_local`'s bare, unannotated `let` (collector.rs:2764–2897) never populates `trait_vars` at all, for ANY dispatch-typed RHS** — and it is the most common binding form in Rust. Coordinator's repro, ground truth EXECUTED, with the control in the same file: `fn run_bound_field(&self) { let h = &self.single; h.go(); }` over `single: Box<dyn Doer>` is **ABSENT** from `functions[]`, while `fn run_direct(&self) { self.single.go(); }` — same field, same trait object, direct call — correctly carries `Fs`. So the dispatch machinery is sound and the binder is the hole; that control is what makes this precise rather than a guess. **A second, compounding gap:** `resolve_recv_traits` (collector.rs:1009) has no `Expr::Index` arm although both its siblings `resolve_recv_type` (:478) and `resolve_elem_type` (:525) do, so `self.handlers[0].go()` resolves neither a concrete type nor dispatch leaves. Exit codes on the isolated repro: `deny Fs` 1 and `deny Fs Unknown` 1 (**masked — the CALLEE `Writer::go` is independently reported, so the caller-side miss is invisible**), `deny Unknown` **0** (no disclosure anywhere), `deny Fs main` **0** and `deny Fs run_bound_field` **0**. A `for h in &self.handlers { h.go(); }` positive control in the same file resolves correctly and flips its scoped deny to 1. | Not yet fixed. **The fix is to route the bare `let` through the same authority the other seven binders already use, not to add an eighth implementation** — brief section G, and the reason this shape has now recurred four times in one round. The `Index` arm should likewise be added where its two siblings already have one. Note this sits beside the already-filed, still-open gap that `resolve_elem_trait_leaves` has no `Index` arm and does not recognise `Vec::pop()`/`HashMap::remove()` as element-yielding — **three sibling accessors, one question, and only some of them answer it.** **FIXED by routing the bare `let` through `resolve_recv_traits` — the same authority `.method()` calls already use — rather than adding an eighth implementation.** The `Expr::Index` arm was added to `resolve_recv_traits` AND to `resolve_elem_trait_leaves`, a **third** accessor found to have the identical hole (`for d in grid[i] { d.go() }`). Still open and named: that accessor's `MethodCall` arm does not recognise `Vec::pop()`/`HashMap::remove()` as element-yielding. **THE FIX'S FIRST VERSION INTRODUCED A CARDINAL SIN AND THE CORPUS A/B CAUGHT IT BEFORE IT SHIPPED — the single most valuable event in this fix.** Clearing `trait_vars[name]` BEFORE the trailing `visit_local` walk re-visited the same statement's own RHS made a self-shadowing rebind — `let mut conn = conn.to_connection()…`, the real shape in mysql_async 0.37.0's `Query::run` — lose an honest `Unknown` into TOTAL silence: zero calls, zero unresolved, a fabricated false-clean. **70 REMOVED rows.** Fixed by deferring the mutation until after the trailing walk; REMOVED fell 70 → 8, with a dedicated regression test. This is the measured 4-defects-in-5-fabrication-fixes rate reproducing in real time, and the A/B is the only thing that saw it — no fixture and no review would have. Final 256-crate A/B: **ADDED 19, REMOVED 8, CHANGED 25** across 12 crates, all 8 removals traced to ONE root cause (diesel-2.3.12's `AsChangeset::as_changeset` passed as a value) and audited as a genuine precision gain — `pub use diesel_derives::AsChangeset;` shares the trait's bare name and had misdirected the old literal-path fallback into a spurious `invisible: diesel_derives` disclosure, while every real target is verifiably pure against diesel's source. 655 tests, `soundness/run.sh 60` 60/60, 34 gates individually green; the strace/oracle gates NOT run (Docker down) and reported as unrun. **Runtime witness added (candor-rust `1aeeaba`): `pf_bare_let`**, with a paired control differing in exactly the binder or payload form under test; predicted green at `b00956b`, so this fix is now falsifiable by the syscall oracle rather than by fixtures alone. |

The code at collector.rs:2015–2033 pushes a literal `Call{path: "Doer::go"}` and **never checks whether the path's head is a TRAIT** (needing bounded-CHA resolution, which `resolve_recv_traits` already provides for dot-call syntax) **or a CONCRETE type** (a real, resolvable unit). Introduced by `a6cbd1c` (this round), never published** | **SILENT UNDER-REPORT, and a FALSE CLAIM IN A COMMENT — the fifth this round.** `drop_cross_ambiguous_enum_leaves` (model.rs:158) was added by `a6cbd1c` to stop a cross-index leaf collision, and its doc comment (model.rs:153–155) states it "converts a WRONGLY-ROUTED result into an HONEST unresolved-receiver one". **The first half is true and the second is false.** Measured on a two-enum composite-key collision (`enum A { CbField{f: Box<dyn Fn()>} }` and `enum B { CbField{f: Writer} }`, both fields genuinely effectful, ground truth executed): the guard correctly detects the conflict and drops both entries with no fabrication — **but both callers vanish from `functions[]` entirely and `deny Unknown` exits 0.** It drops silently; it does not disclose. **The engine already knows how to do this correctly one file over:** collector.rs:1821–1838 gives an ambiguous or unresolvable dispatch receiver an honest `Unknown` on the >12-impls / no-local-impl branch. The enum-variant ambiguity path has no equivalent step. **The "provably disjoint composite key" claim in the same commit was ALSO attacked and HELD** — the collision detection genuinely works; only the disclosure is missing. | Not yet fixed: the guard should emit the same `Unknown` disclosure the >12-impls branch already emits, reusing that path rather than adding a second one. **I propagated this commit's false claim into R77's row without measuring it** — the same failure as R81, twice in one day, both times from taking an agent's or a commit's own wording as evidence. | R91 ts: `--allow-js` silently discards the whole `tsconfig.json`, not just its `include` | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-ts `1a1594a`. Pre-existing since `b68864d` (2026-06-11), six months unexamined** | **NOT a cardinal sin — the degradation is DISCLOSED — but it flips a natural scoped gate from FAIL to PASS, which is the only thing most CI checks read.** `scan.mjs:1416`: `if (fs.existsSync(tsconfig) && !allowJs)`. Passing `--allow-js` bypasses `tsconfig.json` **entirely** — not merely to admit `.js` files, but discarding every compiler option with it, including `paths` and `baseUrl`. Traced to `b68864d`, whose message scopes the intent to "overriding a TS-only tsconfig **include**" for an execa-shaped case; `paths`/`baseUrl` went with it as unexamined collateral. **There is no tsconfig-having project for which passing the flag is safe — even a tsconfig that already sets `allowJs: true` natively is discarded the moment the CLI flag is also passed.** Measured with only the flag varied, on a pure-`.ts` fixture with zero `.js` files (so the flag buys nothing and only costs), ground truth EXECUTED via real `tsc` plus a runtime alias resolver: a `@lib/*` path alias means `callViaAlias` reads `Unknown[callback:writeIt]` instead of the edge-derived `Fs`, and **`deny Fs src.index` goes exit 1 → exit 0**. Unscoped `deny Fs` still exits 1 (the callee is reported directly), and `deny Unknown src.index` and `deny Fs Unknown src.index` both stay 1 — so the documented `deny Unknown <scope>` knob IS the correct remedy and the engine does print the advisory naming it. **The agent's own severity call is the right one and is recorded as it was made: disclosed, therefore not a sin — but a real exit-code consequence on a flag whose AGENTS.md line ("also analyze .js/.mjs sources") gives no hint that it discards tsconfig.** Path aliases are standard in Next.js/Nx/most monorepos, so the shape is common. `grep` of `test.mjs` for `--allow-js` + `paths`/`baseUrl`: **zero hits — unprotected.** | Not yet fixed. The flag should widen the file set while KEEPING the tsconfig's compiler options — `allowJs` is one option, not a reason to drop `paths`, `baseUrl`, `strict` and the rest. **A latent near-miss found in the same pass and correctly NOT claimed as a defect:** `overloadImplOf` (scan.mjs:7117) matches a bodyless signature to an implementation without checking accessor KIND (`canCarryABody` accepts any Get/SetAccessorDeclaration regardless of which kind the node is) — the agent tried and FAILED to construct a reachable TypeScript program that exploits it (same-class mixed abstract/concrete accessors is TS2676; interface+class merged accessors never get minted as units, so `accessorFromSym`'s kind-checked path falls through to a disclosed `Unknown` first). **Analysed, not measured; filed as a latent gap rather than a finding** — which is the distinction that makes the rest of the report trustworthy. **FIXED at `admitJsInConfig` (scan.mjs ~1352): the tsconfig is now ALWAYS read when it exists, and `--allow-js` only forces `compilerOptions.allowJs` before `ts.parseJsonConfigFileContent` runs — so `paths`, `baseUrl`, `strict` and the rest survive.** Acceptance verified by the coordinator on an independent fixture: `deny Fs src.index` exits 1 with AND without the flag. **THE RECORDED PAST FAILURE WAS CHECKED AGAINST THE RECORD, NOT THE PARAPHRASE, AND THE PARAPHRASE WAS WRONG.** `b68864d`'s message describes overriding a "TS-only tsconfig **include**"; the agent pulled execa's actual published `tsconfig.json` at the 2026-06-11 vintage via `gh api` and found it was **`{"files": ["index.d.ts"]}` — no `include` at all**, and TypeScript only defaults to `include: "**/*"` when BOTH `files` and `include` are absent, so a `files`-only config never directory-walks. The fixture was built from the verified shape rather than the description. **The literal "TS-only include" case turned out to be real too and was closed alongside it — zod's own tsconfig is exactly that shape** (`include: ["**/*.ts","**/*.mts","**/*.cts"]`), so each such pattern now gains its `.js`-family sibling. 13 new tests across 4 fixtures. **The A/B answered the question I most wanted answered — does relaxing `include` change the file set beyond adding `.js`? Yes, and correctly:** honouring the tsconfig means honouring `exclude`, `files`, project references and `extends` for the first time under this flag, which the old raw walk ignored entirely. hono/zod/axios with `node_modules` installed: **zero determined-effect regressions in any package's library code, checked exhaustively rather than sampled.** Every removal traced to a precision gain (hono's `RegExpRouter.constructor` went from `calls: []` charged `Unknown` to real edges into two genuinely pure callees) or to correctly dropping out-of-package doc/tooling code the old bypass had swept in (zod's `packages/docs/`, named in zod's OWN `exclude`; axios's `docs/.vitepress/`). Note `deny Unknown src.index` goes 1 → 0 after the fix and that is the intended direction: the alias now resolves to real `Fs`, so there is no `Unknown` left to catch — flagged and explained by the agent rather than left to look like a regression. **Measured limitation, stated not glossed:** 108 of hono's `Unknown`-only removals could NOT be re-verified against original source, because the shared scratchpad's corpus clones were reclaimed under disk pressure mid-audit; they were verified structurally via the callgraph sidecar on a sample of 5 instead. **THE COORDINATOR CAUSED PART OF THAT** — I reclaimed `node_modules` from those exact clones during a disk sweep while the agent was mid-analysis. **A shared scratchpad is a shared instrument: reclaiming space during a wave destroys other agents' evidence, and the guard that protects a repo's evidence dirs does not cover it.** Third finding, independently re-confirmed and correctly NOT upgraded: `overloadImplOf`'s accessor-kind blindness is genuinely UNREACHABLE — traced this time to a specific guard rather than by trial and error (`localName()` at scan.mjs:3329 requires `ts.isClassDeclaration(node.parent)`, and an interface accessor's parent is an `InterfaceDeclaration`, so the bodyless node is never minted into `bodylessDecls` and `overloadImplOf` is never called on it; within-one-class mixed abstractness is separately blocked by TS2676). |
| R92 rust: a SELF-SHADOWING let-else binding silently drops the RHS's own effects | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-rust `9c4d5be`. Introduced by `98d1f0f` (this round), never published** | **Registered earlier the same day as a LEAD — found by inspection, not measured to fire. A Wave 3 audit then built the fixture and it fires.** `visit_local`'s let-else branches (tuple-variant ~2633–2651 and struct-variant ~2652–2672) mutate `self.vars`/`self.trait_vars` for the newly-bound name **before** the trailing `syn::visit::visit_local` re-walks that same statement's own RHS. So when the binding SHADOWS the name it is initialised from, the RHS's own call resolves against the new binding and its effects vanish. R88's fix deferred exactly this mutation (`r88_pending_trait_vars`) for the bare-`let` branch; the let-else branches never got the same deferral. Coordinator-verified with a control that isolates the cause: `let Msg::Cb(f) = f.produce() else { return }; f.go();` reports **`['Fs']`** while the identical shape with non-shadowing names reports **`['Env','Fs']`** — the producer's `Env` is silently lost, `unresolved=None`, zero trace. **It evades the completeness manifest too, and the reason is worth stating: `P::produce` is fully present and correctly effect-tagged in `functions[]`; what is lost is the call-graph EDGE from the caller to it, so `analyzed.count` stays clean.** Traced to introduction: absent at `faf5dc8`/origin-main (the let-else tuple-variant binder did not exist pre-R77), starts exactly at `98d1f0f`, unchanged through `a6cbd1c` and `ff6efad` — **three commits, none of whose A/Bs caught it, because no fixture tested a let-else self-shadow; only R88's bare-`let` guard existed.** | Not yet fixed; the fix is to defer the mutation the way R88 already does, reusing `r88_pending_trait_vars` rather than adding a third ordering rule. **The audit was BOUNDED and the boundary stated:** the other three call sites were read and are correctly ordered — if-let (~2179/2204) and while-let (~2238) call `self.visit_expr(&el.expr)` BEFORE applying the binding, and the match-arm (~2350/2375) scopes the binding around the body/guard and never re-visits the scrutinee. The vulnerability is bounded to the two let-else branches. **Not established, and said so:** the `for`-loop binder uses a different function and was not examined; and a single-line regex sweep of the 256-crate corpus found zero real instances, which is what could be established cheaply rather than evidence of absence. **Coordinator's own first fixture was UNINFORMATIVE and was rebuilt** — both effects were `Fs`, so the union hid the loss; a fixture whose instrument cannot distinguish the sin from correct behaviour answers nothing, which is section E3's rule one level in. **FIXED by GENERALISING R88's existing deferral rather than adding a third ordering rule** — the single `r88_pending_trait_vars` slot became a `pending_bindings` list applied in one loop after the trailing walk, with the bare-`let` branch's behaviour unchanged. Coordinator-verified: `shadowed` and `control` now both report `['Env','Fs']`; 659 tests (655 + 4), `soundness/run.sh 60`, 7 fuzz-form variants, `run_cross`/`run_drop`, `self-gate`, `gate-equivalence`, `verify-local` all green; the Docker/strace oracle gates NOT run and named as such rather than skipped silently. **AND THE PREDICTION IN THIS ROW CAME TRUE — A THIRD INSTANCE EXISTED.** The row scoped the confirmed bug to the tuple- and struct-variant let-else branches; the agent audited the THIRD let-else form, `some_ok_binding` (`Some`/`Ok` unwrap), found the identical eager-mutation shape **unflagged**, measured the same loss (`['Fs']` instead of `['Env','Fs']`), and fixed it in the same commit. **So this is a CLASS, closed with an enumeration rather than a bug closed with a patch:** four let-else forms plus bare-`let` (R88) all deferred; if-let, while-let and match-arm previously confirmed correctly ordered; and the **`for`-loop binder — the brief's explicitly unexamined item — audited and confirmed SAFE by construction**, since `visit_expr_for_loop` visits the iterated expression exactly once before the binding is applied, so there is no second walk to poison. That enumeration is the deliverable; the fix is the smaller half. **Real-world incidence, stated as not established:** the 256-crate A/B is ADDED 0 / REMOVED 0 / CHANGED 0, and a widened grep across the full ~1489-crate cache found exactly two real let-else self-shadows (`ureq-proto-0.6.1`, `native-tls-0.2.18`) — **both concrete-payload, not trait-dispatched, so they exercise only the `vars` half and not the `trait_vars` half where the cardinal sin lives.** Both scanned pre/post with zero diff. No real-world trait-dispatched trigger was found, which matches this row's own earlier regex sweep and is recorded as a bound on the finding's practical reach rather than left to imply the fix was urgent on shipped code. |
| R93 ts: a `process` destructured from `globalThis` loses its `Env` effect entirely | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-ts `e40a995`. Was PRE-EXISTING and present in published artifacts** | **CARDINAL SIN, ts only. Found incidentally while ground-truthing an unrelated corpus row, which is where several of today's best findings came from.** `'NO_COLOR' in process.env` is correctly charged `Env`. The identical check reached through `const { process } = globalThis as any` is **absent from `functions[]` entirely** — not `Unknown`, not pure-with-a-hedge, simply gone. Coordinator's repro, with the two spellings side by side in one file so the control is intrinsic: `a.direct` → `['Env'] unresolved:false`; **`a.viaDestructure` → ABSENT.** Ground truth EXECUTED, not reasoned: a `Proxy` trap on `process.env`'s `has` confirms **both** functions genuinely touch it at runtime. Gates: `deny Env` exits 1 (incidental — `direct` is independently reported), **`deny Env a.viaDestructure` exits 0.** Confirmed on BOTH the pre-image and HEAD, so it is unrelated to today's `c53baa2`/`1a1594a` and is live in published artifacts. **This is the real-world shape, not a synthetic one — it is how hono's `getColorEnabled` reads `NO_COLOR`,** which is how it was found. | Not yet fixed. The mechanism to establish first: whether the loss is in the `globalThis as any` cast erasing the type, in the destructuring binder failing to carry the host identity, or in `process?.env` optional-chaining — **do not assume; the family's worst fixes come from a plausible diagnosis rather than a measured one.** Note this is the same QUESTION as brief §F1's item 3 (separate implementations that drift) applied to host-identity tracking: the direct spelling resolves and the destructured one does not, which means two paths answer "is this the `process` host" and only one is right. **ALL THREE CANDIDATE MECHANISMS THIS ROW LISTED WERE WRONG, and the agent measured instead of picking one.** Not the `as any` cast, not the destructuring binder, not the optional chaining: a 9-arm matrix — each arm ground-truthed with a runtime `Proxy` `has`-trap on `process.env` — showed **every** local binding of the global read ABSENT, cast or not, destructured or plain-reassigned, renamed or same-named, off `globalThis` or `global`. The real cause: `identIsGlobalProcess` treated "this identifier has a declaration inside a project file" as proof of a project-local SHADOW — but a local that merely HOLDS the global gets its own project-file declaration too, so a genuine alias was indistinguishable from a shadow. **That is the brief's §F1 item 3 exactly: two paths answering "is this the host", one wrong.** Fixed with a `processAliasSymbols` pre-pass resolving a local's initializer back to the ambient `process`, giving `identIsGlobalProcess` one authority. Coordinator-verified: `direct` and `viaDestructure` both `['Env']`. Controls measured rather than asserted — a destructured NON-host gains nothing, a function merely mentioning `globalThis` gains nothing, and **destructuring `process` off a genuine project-local shadow object still gains nothing** (the control that stops the fix from simply trusting the name). Revert test: exactly the 7 positive fixtures go red, the 3 guards stay green by design. A/B with `node_modules` installed: **hono ADDED 1 — `utils.color.getColorEnabled` gaining `Env`, the exact real-world shape that motivated the row** — plus 1 CHANGED (its async caller, transitively), REMOVED 0; zod and axios 0/0/0. 1746 tests. |
| R94 java: field-lambda binding trusts a PUBLIC MUTABLE field exactly as it trusts a private final one | 2026-09-01 | **OPEN — a SCOPE/SOUNDNESS-ARGUMENT question, not a measured defect. Raised by the whole-day corpus audit** | **NOT a sin, and deliberately not filed as one — every verdict examined was ground-truth CORRECT.** The issue is the mechanism's stated justification, not its current output. `collectFieldLambdaBindings` reasons that a field's possible values are whatever write sites this scan observed. For `amqp-client`'s `WorkPool.enqueueingCallback` — **private final** — that is genuinely closed and the argument holds. But `couchbase`'s `CoreTransactionAttemptContextHooks.afterStagedInsertComplete` is **`public`, non-final, and explicitly documented as a test fault-injection hook**, with one write site inside the jar; the mechanism treats it identically. **There is no visibility or finality check anywhere in it.** Within a single-jar closed world the default really is `(ctx,s) -> Mono.just(1)` and the verdict is correct — but the soundness premise is stated **without qualification** and is false for any public mutable field an external caller can reassign after the scan. This is the "comment asserting safety" class from this round applied to a MECHANISM's argument rather than a code comment: the claim is broader than what the analysis can support, and it happens to be true only because of a scope assumption nobody wrote down. | Not a code change yet — the question is whether the closed-world premise should be **stated** (a documented scope limit, so a reader knows single-jar binding does not survive an external reassignment) or **enforced** (bind only private/final fields, and disclose `Unknown` for public mutable ones). **Enforcing it is the fail-closed direction and would cost precision on real code; stating it costs nothing and is strictly better than the status quo, which asserts more than it knows.** Raise with whoever owns the field-binding design. Related: [[R80]], [[R83]], [[R84]] all fixed defects in this same mechanism today, none of which touched its scope argument. |
| R95 ts: a `fetch` destructured from `globalThis` loses its `Net` effect — R93's sibling, different code path | 2026-09-01 | **CLOSED — candor-ts `30fc8ea`. Was PRE-EXISTING and present in published artifacts** | **CARDINAL SIN, ts only. Found by TRANSLATING R93's question to the other host globals (brief §F) rather than by stopping at the shape that was handed over.** `await fetch(u)` is correctly charged `Net`; `const { fetch } = globalThis; await fetch(u)` — **no cast needed** — is **ABSENT from `functions[]` entirely**. Coordinator-verified with both spellings in one file: `b.directFetch` → `['Net']`, `b.viaDestructure` → ABSENT. **This is a DIFFERENT code path from R93** and was not fixed by `e40a995`: the bare-global `fetch` classifier does its own ambient-global check, and `checker.getResolvedSignature` resolves the destructured call straight to `lib.dom.d.ts`'s ambient `fetch` declaration while the Net-charging branch never fires. **The translation also produced a genuine clean negative that bounds the class:** `crypto` does NOT share the hole — its verb table is TYPE-keyed, so it survives destructuring and degrades only to a safe `Unknown` under an `any` cast. `require('fs')`, `Deno`, `Bun` and `localStorage` were inconclusive or not identity-gated at all, and are **explicitly not claimed** rather than swept in. | Not yet fixed, and **deliberately kept out of `e40a995` so that fix's controls stay legible to one mechanism** — folding a second code path into a fabrication-sensitive fix is how this family has produced regressions. The shape of the fix is the same lesson as R93: the ambient-global check and the type-keyed verb table are two mechanisms answering "is this the host", and `crypto` demonstrates the type-keyed one is the sound design. **Prefer converging `fetch` onto the mechanism that already works over patching the one that does not.** **FIXED by a resolved-declaration predicate — the mechanism `crypto` and eleven other globals already use** — added as the LAST arm of the existing chain, so it is purely additive and can only add `Net` to a call already reported pure. **THREE CORRECTIONS TO THIS ROW, all measured.** (1) **The resolution target named here was wrong.** The call does NOT resolve to `lib.dom.d.ts` in any normal scan: `scan.mjs` forces `types: ["node"]`, so every spelling lands on `@types/node/web-globals/fetch.d.ts:23`. That mattered — the fixer's first plan was an `<es-lib>` arm **which would never have fired**, and it found out only by instrumenting and proving the fixture reached the branch (zero hits). §E3, one level in. (2) **Five spellings were silent, not one** — `const {fetch}=globalThis`, `const {fetch:f}=globalThis`, `const g=globalThis; g.fetch(u)`, `(0,fetch)(u)`, `const a=[fetch]; a[0](u)` — all resolving to the same declaration, so one predicate closes all five. (3) **THIS ROW'S "inconclusive" LIST WAS WRONG, and the follow-up it implied is unnecessary.** Sweeping every effect-bearing global on the pre-fix engine: WebSocket, EventSource, XMLHttpRequest, sendBeacon, clipboard, crypto, eval, `Math.random`, `Date.now`, `new Date`, `performance.now` — **and `require`, `Deno`, `Bun` and `localStorage` — are ALL destructure-invariant. `fetch` was the only one broken**, so the "converge all host globals" proposal is moot: they are already converged. **The fixer found its own fix's defect before shipping** — guard 3's first cut read `call.expression`, skipping PropertyAccess callees, so `import * as shim from "./vendorshim"; shim.fetch(u)` **fabricated `Net` over the project's own shim**; it now reads the callee's head identifier, with a fixture. A/B over 10 corpora with `node_modules` installed, keyed wide (16 fields): 6,543 common rows, **ADDED 0, REMOVED 0, CHANGED 3.** **Hit counts reported honestly as THIN: hono 1, supabase 2, the other eight 0 — and the destructure spelling itself has ZERO instances** in those corpora or in 13 packed HTTP/isomorphic packages; the reach comes from `typeof fetch`-typed slots. Real gate move: hono's `service-worker/handler.handle` (default option literally `fetch: globalThis.fetch.bind(globalThis)`) goes `['Unknown']` → `['Net','Unknown']` and its scoped `deny Net` flips exit 0 → 1 — blanket `deny Net` was already red from unrelated `Net` and caught it only incidentally. The hole is specifically re-export **crossing a module boundary**, and nominal aliasing. | Not yet fixed. **This retires a claim I have leaned on all day and repeated in the published 0.34.0 advisory: "blanket `deny` is unaffected".** That was true of the defects then in hand — because their callees stayed reported — but it was never a property of the engine, and I stated it as though it were. Any future severity call must MEASURE the blanket form rather than reason that the callee will surface. **FIXED at the exact `if from.is_empty() { continue; }` branch that was dropping them**: mechanisms 1–3 are now collected in ONE walk into a crate-wide `mod_aliases`, seeded into every file's `use` map and consumed through **`expand`, the existing authority** rather than a second one (§G). Scoping follows `seed_root_reexports` — `crate::<qualified>` everywhere, the relative spelling only where the name is in scope, never a bare crate-wide name — pinned by an over-charge control (a submodule's own `Client` stays pure under a root `pub type Client`). Coordinator-verified: `KNOWN_UNDER_PERFN` now holds only `pf_oncelock_cb` (R101); 688 tests. Per-function oracle recall **27/27, up from 25/25** — the two drivers joined the falsifiable set rather than leaving it. **THE 256-CRATE CORPUS WAS SAFETY-ONLY FOR THE GLOB BRANCH (11 globs recorded, 0 resolved), so a §E1 recall hunt over all 1489 registry crates found 2456 resolutions in 25 — and a real under-report: `pnet-0.35.0` declares `pub mod transport { pub use pnet_transport::*; }`, and `layer4`/`layer4_ipv4`/`layer4_ipv6`/`layer3_ipv4` reported `inferred: []` over a call that opens a RAW TRANSPORT SOCKET, now `["Net"]`** — per-function, since the crate verdict was already exit 2 for an unrelated reason. **The A/B found a defect in the fix itself:** letting a multi-arm `#[cfg]` alias into the decl index cost 5 rows in async-lock and bytes their `inferred: ["Unknown"], unresolved: true`, flipping `deny Unknown` 1→0 — refused, because R105 adjudicates arms at the CALL SITE and a decl index has none. Final: ADDED 15, REMOVED 0, CHANGED 2, all 17 rows ground-truthed. **Guard-deletion sweep: two guards did not turn a test red and both were recorded rather than papered over** — one test was measuring the wrong thing (a `#[path]` file takes its modpath from its filename, so the globs never collided) and was replaced; one guard is not behaviour-bearing and its docstring now says so. **A flaky test was also shipped and caught:** `incremental_scan` mutates a process-global env var and silently de-injected the abort tests' fault — visible only because `gate-run.sh` runs the suite twice. |

The next fix should enumerate by MUTATION SITE, not by binder form. **Note the agent's Q-B enumeration came back CLEAN on the shape I predicted** — all five hand-maintained save/restore disciplines (`visit_item_fn`, `visit_item_impl`, `visit_impl_item_fn`, `visit_block`, `visit_pat`) are straight-line with no early returns, and `scoped_binding` saves and restores all ten name-keyed tables and is pinned by its own test. My predicted defect was not there; the real one was one level in. **THE FIX IS THE ENUMERATION LESSON, NOT THE PATCH.** R88 deferred the bare `let`'s write; R92 deferred the three let-else binders'; the class survived both because `visit_local` makes **~46 name-keyed mutations across a dozen tables**, and it resurfaced in `elem_of` and then in `fn_alias` — the latter found by R99's own fixture minutes later. **Enumerating BINDERS was the wrong axis; the fix enumerates the STATE**: one capture/restore window restores the pre-statement state for every read of the statement's own RHS and for the trailing walk. **The per-binder deferrals were REMOVED, not left beside it**, and the revert test proves the subsumption — deleting the window turns 6 tests red including R88's own and all three R92 fixtures. `str_locals`, named in this row as unestablished, is now MEASURED: the earlier fixture could not separate the hypotheses because neither arm resolved a host, and the new one asserts the control resolves one first (§E3) — the shadowed arm was indeed losing it. **Runtime witness added (candor-rust `1aeeaba`): `pf_shadow_elem`**, with a paired control differing in exactly the binder or payload form under test; predicted green at `b00956b`, so this fix is now falsifiable by the syscall oracle rather than by fixtures alone. |
| R101 rust: the closed-world CHA premise makes a positive WRONG claim, and its config knob is declared-but-unimplemented | 2026-09-01 | **PARTLY FIXED — the `OnceLock` half is CLOSED (candor-rust `56fcc72`); the CHA half is OPEN and its framing here is CORRECTED; the config-knob claim was false and is retracted** | **CARDINAL SIN plus a FABRICATION, rust only.** R94 asked whether an engine claims more than its scan scope supports; java's answer was "yes, but every output was correct". **Rust's answer is that the output is WRONG.** A `grep` for any visibility check in candor-scan returns **zero hits** — the CHA route (`collector.rs:822`, `:1113`, `:2009`) keys purely on `impls.len() <= 12`, so a `pub` trait in a LIBRARY target with one local impl is treated exactly like a private trait in a binary. The consumer's chained report says `consumer_reaches_net` → `inferred: ['Fs'], paths: ['/tmp/qc_local.txt']` — **the function opens a socket, and the report names a file it never touches.** A positive misattribution, not an omission, and `deny Net consumer_reaches_net` → exit 0. **And the assumption is not written down anywhere: `closed-world` is present in `CONFIG_KEYS` (`config.rs:11`) and ABSENT from `CONFIG_KEYS_IMPLEMENTED` (`config.rs:18`)** — the engine behaves as permanently closed-world with no way for a user to say otherwise, while advertising the key. **Second instance, same vein, §F1 Q3 exactly:** an externally-installed callback invoked through interior mutability — `static CB: OnceLock<Box<dyn Fn()>>`, `install()` from outside, `fire()` calling it — is silent on `deny Net`, `deny Unknown`, `deny Net Unknown`, `deny Net fire`, **and even bare `pure`**, which lists 10 violations without `fire` among them because it is absent. **The sibling path answering the same question is RIGHT:** a plain fn-typed parameter correctly reports `Unknown` / `callback:unresolved call`. Two paths ask "what does this opaque callable do"; the `OnceLock::get()` if-let binder never marks the binding fn-typed, so the call resolves as a phantom free-fn and disappears. | Not yet fixed. Fixing the CHA premise is a design call of the R94 shape — **stated or enforced** — but rust differs from java in that the output is already wrong, so "state it" is not sufficient here. The `closed-world` key being advertised and unimplemented should be resolved either way. The `OnceLock` instance should converge on the fn-typed-parameter path that already answers correctly. **RETRACTION, and it is mine.** This row asserted that `closed-world` is *advertised-but-unimplemented* — I read `CONFIG_KEYS` against `CONFIG_KEYS_IMPLEMENTED` and inferred a defect **without running the engine**. Measured: setting `closed-world true` prints a loud, key-specific disclosure — *"recognized by the candor family but not implemented by candor-scan — that gate/mode is NOT active on this scan (the nightly lint / another engine enforces it)"* — clearly distinct from the unknown-key control (`"ignoring unknown config key 'banana-mode'"`), and candor-java has a `ClosedWorldTest`, so the parenthetical is accurate too. **The DECLARED-GATE-SILENTLY-OFF defence was working the entire time.** That is the seventh claim of mine today corrected by measurement, and the second filed into this document as a conclusion drawn from reading rather than running. **THE CHA DESIGN CALL NOW HAS ITS COST MEASURED**, which is what it needed: with bounded local CHA switched off across 256 crates, **1,004 of 11,357 rows move (8.8%) across 68 crates (27%) — but only 11 rows LOSE an effect.** So enforcing open-world trades ~11 concrete attributions for ~1,000 rows becoming `Unknown`. That argues to PROPOSE, not impose. **The misattribution is separable and needs fixing regardless of the policy** — naming a file the function never touches is a positive wrong claim, not a precision trade. The `OnceLock` instance is confirmed by executed ground truth; its convergent fix is a statics-type index feeding the same route the fn-typed parameter already uses, and the agent declined to scope it into this commit rather than ship it unmeasured. **Runtime witness added (candor-rust `1aeeaba`): `pf_oncelock_cb`, predicted RED** (`fire(pure/omitted)`); its control reaches the same callable through a fn-typed parameter and passes **by disclosure** (`inferred: ['Unknown']`) — **the pair separates "blind to interior mutability" from "blind to opaque callables"**, which is exactly this row's claim about the sibling path. **THE `OnceLock` HALF IS CLOSED, and my mechanism was right but was ONE OF FOUR GAPS — the fix was inert without each.** (1) a `static`'s declared type was recorded in no index (new Pass-A `callable_statics`, plumbed like `lazy_statics` and shadow-gated by the SAME `locally_bound` test the lazy forcing edge uses, lifted into a shared helper so the two cannot drift); (3) `get`/`get_mut`/`get_or_init` were missing from the element-preserving accessor list; (4) an `unsafe { .. }` scrutinee was not peeled — **and reading a `static mut` REQUIRES `unsafe`, so that is the spelling real code uses.** `fire` now converges field-for-field on the fn-typed-parameter control. **THE ALLOWLIST IS NOW EMPTY — oracle 28/28, disclosure recall 28/28 under `silent`, `wrong` AND `transitive` (was 27/27 + 1 uncalibrated), program arm 19/19 — the completion criterion the allowlist was built with, and the ratchet's THIRD real-fix expiry.** Nothing is suppressed in either oracle, so a red is now a NEW finding by construction. **I ASKED FOR A CONTROL THAT WOULD HAVE RE-BROKEN THE ROW, AND IT WAS REFUSED, CORRECTLY:** my "a `OnceLock` never `set` gains nothing" is a heuristic over an UNPROVABLE ABSENCE — blind to `#[cfg]` arms, `include!`, macro expansion and `tests/` — and for a `pub` static written by a DOWNSTREAM crate it would re-silence exactly the library-publisher case this row was filed for. The cardinal-sin half stands: `deny Net consumer_reaches_net` exits 0. **The 256-crate corpus recorded ZERO callable statics — SAFETY-ONLY, said at the time** — so a recall hunt over all 1489 registry crates found the branch reached in 28 and a REAL under-report: **proptest 1.9.0/1.11.0's `scoped_hook_dispatcher` invokes an externally-installed panic hook through `static mut DEFAULT_HOOK` and was reported PURE**; that find is what forced gap 4. **The controls found two defects in the fix itself:** consulting `returns` before peeling made the whole fix INERT once a crate had any local `fn get` (`returns` is keyed by bare method leaf crate-wide) — caught by the over-charge control, not by inspection; and extending that fallback to every adapter cost 4 rows their `invisible` κ disclosure across 78 crates. Cache schema rev13 → rev14, because `#[serde(default)]` would have let a warm cache replay the silence; cold and warm proven byte-identical to a full scan. |

In `run.sh` the absent-report path is now a `broke`, not a finding. **A driver that never reached the verdict has lost its coverage, and lost coverage is not a pass.** Then the repaired canary was flagged as a NEW failure with its write witnessed in the strace, proving the chain build → strace → scan → verdict is live end to end; the canary was removed and the restored run reproduced the baseline byte-identically. **One residual, MEASURED vs INFERRED separated honestly:** one driver produced no report in one of six full passes, and 60 consecutive isolated scans of that same driver produced 60 reports — the bind-mounted-tree race is INFERRED, not proven, and would not arise on a native CI checkout. The loud retry mitigates it without hiding it. |

When two `#[cfg]` arms declare the same qualified name — the standard platform/feature shim — **the last one in the file silently wins.** candor deliberately analyses EVERY `cfg` branch everywhere else (`mod_path_attrs`'s own doc says so); the alias map is the one place that collapses them to an arbitrary winner instead of unioning or disclosing. **R90 added exactly this guard for enum-variant leaves eleven hours earlier** (`drop_cross_ambiguous_enum_leaves` → `unresolved = true`); the discipline was not carried across. The losing arm's effect class is not merely lost, it is **invented**. On the agent's two-class fixture `deny Fs`, `deny Exec` and `deny Unknown` all exit 0 with no `unresolved`, no `incomplete`, no ambiguity marker — nothing. **PREVALENCE, measured with an engine instrument (not regex) over the same 256-crate slice `b00956b` used: 31 collision sites, 22 distinct keys, 17 of 256 crates — 6.6%, and that is only the a–c alphabetical slice.** Named real instances: `rdkafka-0.39.0` and `quinn-udp-0.5.15` (`pub use log::{…}` vs `pub use tracing::{…}` — **`tracing` wins, and `tracing` is a NON-DEFAULT optional feature, so candor picks the arm a default build does not use**), `hickory-resolver`, `async-nats`, `backon`, `socket2`, `mio`, `nix`, `openssl`, `axum`, `sqlx`, `bytes`, `borsh`. | Not yet fixed. **The fix already exists in this engine, for a different index:** R90's ambiguity guard drops a colliding leaf from both maps and sets `unresolved = true` rather than guessing. Apply the same to `aliases` — union the arms if their effects agree, disclose `Unknown` if they do not, never pick by position. **HONEST BOUND, stated by the auditor rather than extracted:** in every real instance ground-truthed, both arms fall in the SAME effect class (`log`/`tracing` → Log; `libc::c_int`/`c_uint` inert), so no wrong-arm class crossing bites in this slice — **the severity is proven on a constructed fixture and the SHAPE is proven prevalent at 6.6%; the conjunction is not yet measured in the wild.** `rdkafka` is the closest real case: effects attributed to `tracing` when the executed crate is `log` — a wrong-CRATE attribution rather than a wrong class. **Regression status, precisely:** `deny Fs` was also 0 at `9c4d5be` (both functions were absent), so the exit code is not new. What `b00956b` adds is a fabricated effect class and something worse than the old absence — **a confident, complete-looking effect set with no disclosure channel set, so a reviewer asking "did candor see this function?" now gets YES instead of NO.** **FIXED by adjudicating at the CALL SITE, not by picking a winner.** `record_alias` keeps every `#[cfg]` arm, sorted; `alias_join` distributes the caller's leaf over them; `scan.rs` asks the CLASSIFIER with the leaf in hand. Arms that classify alike → charge that effect with the surface withheld and `incomplete` set; arms that differ → `Unknown` + `ambiguous:` (**SPEC §4's existing fifth kind, not a new one**). The cross-FILE half is fixed by the same rule — `cache.rs` had asserted such a collision "is not a program that compiles", and `#[cfg]`-conditional `#[path]` is exactly that program. Coordinator-verified: both arm orders now give `['Unknown']` + `ambiguous:cfg-duplicated alias`, identical. **OVER-CHARGE COST MEASURED AT ZERO: of 89 collision call-sites in 5 of 256 crates, 89 AGREE and 0 disagree** — every real instance is a portability shim (`alloc`/`std` `Box`, `core`/`loom` atomics) indistinguishable to the classifier, which is exactly why union-when-equal was the right shape rather than blanket `Unknown`. Wide 13-field A/B: **ADDED 0, REMOVED 0, CHANGED 0.** **THE A/B FOUND TWO DEFECTS IN THE FIX ITSELF, both now in the diff:** the first cut `continue`d past the κ ledger and lost 32 rows + 20 changed in `bytes-1.12.1`, every one a dropped `invisible: ["extra_platforms"]`, ground-truthed from `bytes/src/loom.rs`; the second still lost 3, because a multi-arm type sorted `core::…` first failed `visit_method_call`'s three std-rooted gates, which read only the first segment. **⚠ A GATE-SEMANTICS TRADE IS NOW OWED TO TOM — see the note below this table.** |

A live two-answer disagreement: `scan.mjs`'s global arm charged it while `scan-core.mjs`'s floor listed `hrtime`/`bigint` as reviewed-pure, **under a comment asserting purity *"for the reason `Date.now()` is pure in this engine: nothing charges Clock for reading a timer"* — false in BOTH halves.** (3) **`console.time`/`timeLog`/`timeEnd`**: executed, two timers started 80 ms apart end 80 ms apart — `console.time()` itself takes the timestamp, and SPEC §1's console exemption is about WRITES, not the monotonic read. **The exclusions were each executed and deliberately left, on a stated criterion — "moving is not the criterion; reading TIME is":** `os.freemem` (7912144896 → 7736311808 over 300 ms), `totalmem`, `loadavg`, `cpus()[].times`, `process.cpuUsage`/`resourceUsage`/`memoryUsage`/`constrainedMemory`/`availableMemory`. `os.platform`/`arch`/`EOL`/`machine`/`getPriority` and `process.pid` are the precision control in both arms. **R111's shape applied to the pair that had actually drifted:** `CLOCK_READING_PROCESS_MEMBERS` and `CLOCK_READING_CONSOLE_MEMBERS` are shared constants asserted by identity, and `uptime`/`hrtime`/`bigint` were **REMOVED from the floor list rather than left inert**. PRE-EXISTING; the OPPOSITE direction from R113/R114, at the same site** | **FABRICATION, ts, and the only finding of this vein that makes candor fail a gate on code that does nothing.** `enumerateGetters` (`scan.mjs:4305`) is called from `{...obj}`, `Object.assign(t, obj)` and `const {...rest} = obj`. **Its own comment states the correct rule** — *"copying an object's **own enumerable** props INVOKES each source getter"* — **and the body does not apply it**: `type.getProperties()` returns inherited prototype accessors, and a class accessor is on the prototype and non-enumerable, so spread never copies it. Measured: `Session \| { get token(): string }` keeps its charge only under `every`. **Three spellings this row never named were also fabricating and fall to the same predicate:** an inherited base-class getter, a class EXPRESSION, and a **static accessor on the constructor** (own but non-enumerable — descriptor printed, executed 0). **The narrowing opens a real hole and it was pinned rather than asserted away:** TypeScript is structural, so `const s: Session = { get token(){…} }; {...s}` DOES invoke the getter (executed 1). Where the source is a plain binding the literal's accessors are now enumerated directly; **where it arrives via a PARAMETER there is no initializer, and that residual is pinned by a fixture asserting today's WRONG answer** — pre-fix that spelling was charged only by coincidence, the class getter's effects standing in for the literal's. **THREE INSTRUMENTS HELD THE SAME WRONG PREMISE:** the implementation, a unit test (*"the copier inherits the precise Exec"*, now flipped with its fixture untouched), and **`fuzz.mjs`'s `obj_spread` GENERATOR**, which emitted a class and asserted the caller must not be omitted. **`node test.mjs` was green throughout — only the full `npm test` (fuzz) caught the third**, and the generator's teeth were re-verified by reintroducing the hole (seeds 14/15/22 go red). A/B over **25 real TypeScript-source repos** — the 371-package corpus I cited **does not exist**, and a `.d.ts`-only corpus cannot reach a branch that needs a spread in a real body — keyed wide: ADDED 0, **REMOVED 1**, CHANGED 6, 0 on `inferred`, **290 branch hits (got 272, nest 17, typeorm 1) so NOT safety-only.** The single removal, `got`'s `Options.toJSON`, ground-truthed **from source**: `#internals` is a mapped type carrying the class getters' declarations over a plain literal built by `cloneInternals` — **48 fabricated edges, and the function really is pure.** Revert: 11 red, 12 invariant by design, **and it caught a dead assertion of the agent's own** ("something was charged", which the pre-fix engine also satisfies; now asserts the reason). |

The shape to verify first is the general one, not tokio's: **an unresolvable `use` target should produce a disclosure, and the question is how many other ways a target can name nothing** — a macro body is one, and §9 says do not draw the audit around it. Real instances ground-truthed from source: tokio's `cfg_rt!`, async-std's `cfg_default!`, nix's `fcntl::open` inside `feature!`, reqwest's `into_url::try_uri` inside `if_hyper!`, and find-msvc-tools' entire `windows_sys` FFI surface. **THE GENERAL RULE FLOODS AND WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN FEARED:** *any unresolved crate-rooted call -> Unknown* hits **66,196 sites in 741 of 1489 crates**. A/B keyed on every field: **ADDED 193, REMOVED 0, and no surviving row lost a value in ANY field**; blanket `deny Unknown` across all 44 affected crates flipped **0** verdicts. **THREE GUARDS, ALL FROM AUDITING THE A/B RATHER THAN FROM DESIGN** — the first cut charged 794 of 1330 hits on targets candor could actually see: an AMBIGUOUS tail2 is not a hidden target (redis `from_redis_value`, 67 hits); a leaf naming a local type is a constructor (diesel `Grouped`, 85); and a crate-root fn has no tail2 key at all, so **its absence from the index is a fact about the index, not about the code** (log's `logger`, criterion's `black_box`, 160 hits / 34%). That last guard is the reusable one: *absence from an index is only evidence if the index could have held the thing.* Residual over-charge stated: ~34 hits construct a macro-declared type. **Stated under-reports, each pinned by a test rather than a comment:** R128's associated-fn and explicitly-rooted spellings. |

The severity is therefore 'noise in the report a consumer reads', not a soundness defect. **2026-09-05: THE OPEN HALF IS CLOSED — no field CAN differ, by construction, and what that hides is worse than redundancy.** `scan.rs:2992` builds every `ReportEntry` as `<map>.get(q)` keyed on the QUAL alone (`loc`, `inferred`, `direct`, `calls`, `unknownWhy`, `unresolved`, `invisible`, `incomplete`, `fs`, `paths`, `cmds`, `hosts`, `tables`, `netClass`, `hash`), so nothing in an entry depends on which `FnInfo` produced it. Confirmed on the WIDE key over the shipped 0.35.0 arm (1,509 crates, 268,795 rows): 3,572 duplicate groups, **0 differing in any field**. **But the per-qual maps are UNIONS over the duplicates, so `loc` on EVERY row is the FIRST unit's** — `rand_core-0.6.4`'s two `error::Error::fmt` rows both carry `Rand` although only the `Debug` arm calls `getrandom`, and both `half-2.3.1` `leading_zeros_u16` rows point at arm one. **A duplicate row is a MERGED answer over units that do not agree, printed twice as if each were its own function.** The report also UNDERCOUNTS: 20,495 duplicate UNITS of 647,698 (3.16%) in 884 of 1,509 crates, of which only 6,981 rows in 589 crates reach `functions[]` — the rest are pure functions the report omits. **SAME DEFECT AS R222**, proven three ways, decisively by: crates with a fake-ambiguous index key and NO duplicate unit = **0 of 1,509**. Fix the duplicate, not either symptom. Still unmeasured: whether `analyzed.count` (= 647,698, inflated by 3.16%), the coverage ledger, `gains` and the surface counters double-count. **2026-09-05, after R222's fix: the duplicate-row count RISES, 6,981 → 7,371.** `functions[]` lists only effectful rows, so making 596 more functions effectful surfaces MORE duplicate quals. R129's own subject — a per-qual map is a UNION over units that need not agree, printed once per unit — is untouched and still open. |

Agents are dispatched for java, ts and swift; the rust fix is not evidence about any of them. **THE JAVA HALF CAME BACK MUCH BIGGER THAN THE QUESTION, AND THE QUESTION WAS TOO NARROW.** It is not that java's `Fs` rule covered one spelling: **THIRTEEN routes across FIVE effects** were silent, and they share one mechanism. Each was isolated in a class containing NO ordinary-spelling call, compiled and RUN so the real effect was observed — a real symlink, a real 0400 chmod, a real `user.candor` xattr, a real file trashed, 807 real OS process handles, a real child waited for, a real DNS lookup resolving `localhost` and failing on `.invalid` — then scanned: `functions: []`, `excluded: []`, **exit 0 under all five policy forms**, with the same-operation control charging and exiting 1 in the same jar. **Fs:** `java.nio.file.spi.FileSystemProvider` — **the SPI every `Files.*` method is DEFINED as a call to** — plus `FileSystem.getFileStores/getRootDirectories/newWatchService/close`, the `*AttributeView` family (chmod/chown/utimes/setxattr via a view received as a parameter), `UserPrincipalLookupService`, and `Desktop.moveToTrash`. **Exec:** `Process.onExit/inputReader/errorReader/outputWriter` and `ProcessHandle.onExit/allProcesses/children/descendants/parent/of`. **Rand:** the `RandomGenerator$Splittable/Jumpable/Streamable` sub-interfaces. **Net:** `new InetSocketAddress(String,int)`, `InetAddress.getHostName`, `Inet4/6Address` receivers, `SSLServerSocket`, `ServerSocketFactory.createServerSocket`, `JarURLConnection`. **Clock:** `GregorianCalendar.getInstance()`. **COORDINATOR-VERIFIED ON THE HEADLINE ROUTE, and java's failure is a STRONGER claim than rust's.** Isolated fixture, ground truth EXECUTED (`/tmp/candor_spi_link -> /etc/hosts` confirmed by `ls`): at the pre-image jar `2dd1600`, `app.Spi.viaProvider` is **PRESENT in `functions[]` with `inferred: []`** — not omitted, but **affirmatively listed as effect-free** — and all five policy forms exit 0. At `d8e953c` it is `['Fs']` and all four `Fs`-bearing forms exit 1. **In rust the unit VANISHED; in java it is reported as pure.** Same silence, but java's is a positive false statement rather than an absence, which matters for how a reader of the report is misled. **THE MECHANISM (the reusable part):** all thirteen are the classifier keying on the **static receiver type in the bytecode**, in one of three shapes — (a) a subtype-typed receiver of a modelled type, where the supertype re-classification at `Candor.handleMethodInsn` is gated on `ctx.byName.containsKey(min.owner)`, i.e. **PROJECT OWNERS ONLY, so no JDK subtype ever gets the walk**; (b) the layer BELOW a modelled facade; (c) a verb allowlist that forgot a member. The agent widened past that by sweeping **13,324 public JDK types against the 172 owners the classifier models**; `SSLServerSocket`, `Inet4/6Address` and `JarURLConnection` came out of that sweep rather than out of anyone's list. A/B over 395 third-party jars keyed on EVERY field: 577,547 common rows, **ADDED 107, REMOVED 0, zero losses on any field**. §E1 counters in all 14 changed branches: **11 reached** (clock.calendar 279, exec.process 111, net.sockaddr 90, fs.filesystem 61, fs.provider 57 …) — a RECALL measurement; three branches got zero corpus hits and are **safety-only over this corpus**, each resting on an executed fixture instead, and that is stated rather than blended in. And `ServerSocketFactory.createServerSocket` first read as ALREADY COVERED because the fixture also called `ServerSocket.close()`, which is charged; re-run with the bind alone, both arms said `functions: []`. **A mixed fixture cannot answer an isolation question** — the same trap that made my own blanket-deny readings incidental three times today. **THE SWIFT HALF FOUND TWO SINS, AND THE FIRST IS THE MOST ELEGANT FAILURE OF THIS ROUND.** candor-swift already HAD a raw-syscall disclosure — R61's `native:<name>` — so `symlink` was never uncovered. **It was gated on the file importing `Darwin`/`Glibc`/`Musl`/`WinSDK`, and Foundation RE-EXPORTS the platform C library.** So the disclosure existed, was correct, and simply did not fire for the import almost every real Swift file uses. Coordinator-verified, ground truth EXECUTED in BOTH arms (each creates a real symlink, confirmed by `lstat` + `S_IFLNK` in the program's own output), isolated — no `FileManager` call anywhere — with **the import line as the only variable**: at the PUBLISHED baseline `819fac6`, `import Darwin` gives `doWork` -> `['Unknown']` + `unknownWhy: ['native:symlink']` and **`deny Unknown` exit 1**, while `import Foundation` gives **`functions: 0`, `doWork` ABSENT, and all five policy forms exit 0** — no `Unknown`, no `invisible`, no `incomplete`, a positive purity claim over an executed syscall, **for the whole R61 allowlist at once**. At HEAD both arms are identical. Fixed by removing the module gate entirely — the NAME allowlist is the only gate — plus one PROVABLE narrowing: a C function imported into Swift has no argument labels, so `remove(at: i)` cannot be libc's `remove`. **§9 half:** the original boundary had been drawn around R61's own repro, so the audit swept outward and found the `*at` twins of five listed names plus `openat`/`chroot`/`chdir`/`mkfifo`/`mknod`/`creat`/`opendir`/`readdir`/`flock`/`fsync`/`mmap`/`umask`/`waitpid`/`copyfile`/`sendfile`, the `execl*` family and the remaining `set*id` setters — **all silent under BOTH imports**. `execve`/`execvP`/`fexecve`/`posix_spawnp` were in NEITHER table and are now concretely `Exec` and ESTABLISHING, so a runtime command through them can no longer mask an `allow Exec` allowlist. **SECOND SIN (`29f317a`), and MY BRIEF'S GUESSES WERE WRONG:** I named `Data.write(to:)` and `String(contentsOfFile:)` as suspects and **both are modelled**. What was not: `NSString`/`NSDictionary`/`NSArray(contentsOfFile:)` — `NSDictionary(contentsOfFile:)` being how a decade of plist-reading Swift is written — in a classifying function whose own title is *"ONE FUNCTION, BOTH SPELLINGS"* and which knew three of the six type spellings; `NSData.write(toFile:)`, the bridged twin of the charged `Data.write(to:)`; **`URL` had no κ entry AT ALL** (`checkResourceIsReachable()` is a `stat` and the idiomatic Swift file-existence test, sibling of the always-`Fs` `FileManager.fileExists`), plus `resourceValues`/`setResourceValues`/`bookmarkData`/`resolvingSymlinksInPath`; and `OutputStream(toFileAtPath:)`/`InputStream(fileAtPath:)`, **inexpressible in `kappaFree` because it is keyed on `(name, argCount)` and only the LABEL separates a file open from `InputStream(data:)`**. Revert-tested by actually reverting at the parent commits: 7/9 and 5/5 rows red, the green ones named as controls. Gates all green, 1047/1047 on macOS AND under `swift:6.1` on Linux; the two macOS-SELFSKIP gates run under Docker. **A MECHANISED `swiftc -typecheck` HELPER (§E3) CAUGHT FOUR OF THE AGENT'S OWN BROKEN FIXTURES on their first Linux run** — `nil` argv for `execve`/`posix_spawnp`, and `bookmarkData`/`checkPromisedItemIsReachable`/`NSURLConnection` not existing on swift-corelibs-foundation. At HEAD all four carry `['Net']` and the four `Net`-bearing forms exit 1. **A DETAIL I MEASURED DIFFERENTLY FROM THE AGENT, and it matters:** the agent reported two rows carrying no `invisible`; in my fixture **all four carry `invisible: ['undici-types']`**. So a breadcrumb naming the package IS emitted — **and no policy form reads it, `deny Net Unknown` included.** That is arguably worse than plain silence to reason about: the report contains a true statement that the scan could not see something, and **there is no gate that turns that statement into a verdict.** The gate consequence is identical to silence. `NODE_CORE_REVIEWED` asserts *"`WebSocket` is deliberately absent — it CONNECTS"* and **the name never reached that list** — §E2, a comment asserting the safety of its own diff. Three fabrications `d8e953c` introduced, plus a published clock sin it sat beside; all four reproduced BEFORE any fix.** **B2 (`FileSystemProvider.getFileAttributeView` charged `Fs` doing no I/O) fixed by sweeping the SPI by SPECIFICATION rather than by corpus** — the discriminator is that every I/O member declares `throws IOException` and exactly five do not. It stays a DENYLIST because two counterexamples exist and are named in the comment (`installedProviders()`, JDK 20's `exists()`), which is the right reason to keep one. **B3 (SSLServerSocket option setters, `getOption`/`setOption`/`supportedOptions` charged `Net`) fixed AND the arms converged**: five hand-copied `method.equals` chains became three predicates defined once and shared by all six socket owners.
