Both audits re-run. Gates: `test` 953/0, `smoke.sh`, `soundness/run.sh 40`, `reentrancy.sh`, `verify-local.sh` all OK. **`ci/self-gate.sh` RUN BY HAND — exit 0, "no violations"** — because `gate-run.sh` structurally cannot see inside a `run: |` block, and this is the gate from CLAUDE.md's four-red-commits story. `nativeCompile` UNRUN and **verified to fail identically at the merge-base** rather than assumed. |
| R131 java: the receiver-type mechanism behind R130 is NOT fixed — only its thirteen instances | 2026-09-02 | **OPEN — named by the agent as the highest-value open item, with the fabrication risk already measured** | **MECHANISM, java. R130's java half fixed thirteen symptoms; the rule that produced them is unchanged.** The supertype re-classification at `Candor.handleMethodInsn` is gated on `ctx.byName.containsKey(min.owner)` — project owners only — so **no JDK subtype of a modelled type ever gets the supertype walk**. Every future JDK subtype of an already-modelled type is silent by default, which is why this shape has now been patched by hand four times (`MulticastSocket`, `SSLSocket`, Conscrypt, and R130's thirteen). | Not fixed, and **the reason is a measured trade rather than difficulty**. Extending the walk to external owners would catch the next sibling automatically, but it is a hot-path change whose fabrication risk was sampled and NOT A/B'd in budget: `javax.sql.rowset.CachedRowSet` extends `ResultSet` (would charge `Db` on a DISCONNECTED rowset — a fabrication), and `LineNumberReader`, `DataInputStream`, `GZIPInputStream`, `ZipInputStream`, `JarInputStream` and `CipherInputStream` all extend the filter-stream `Unknown` rule (would flood `Unknown`). **Found by the same sweep and filed here rather than fixed, analysis-only:** those `java.io.*` filter-stream subclasses are **silent-pure where the rule INTENDS `Unknown`** — a gap in the opposite direction from the flood risk, in the same types. Also `javax.management.loading.MLet`, `com.sun.net.httpserver.HttpsServer`/`HttpsExchange`, `javax.script.AbstractScriptEngine`, `java.util.prefs.AbstractPreferences`. `sun.nio.fs.*` not examined (needs `--add-exports`). **The decision this needs is not "fix or not" but WHICH DIRECTION TO FAIL IN**, and it is a denylist question ([[candor-denylist-over-allowlist]]): extend the walk and carve out the measured fabrication sources, rather than continue enumerating subtypes by hand — the enumeration has now been wrong four times and each round found the previous boundary drawn around its own trigger. |

| R139 rust: a local `macro_rules!` DEFINITION body is invisible to `count_ident`, so R119's promotion still kills the effect | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-rust `c4571a9`. The counts come out equal, the shadow is promoted function-wide, and the effect inside the expansion is killed. Coordinator-verified, ground truth EXECUTED (`spawned=true` — `/usr/bin/true` really runs): a crate with `macro_rules! spawn_it { ($p:expr) => { Cmd::new($p).status().is_ok() } }`, a nested-block `struct Cmd`, and `spawn_it!(p)` reads **`['Exec']` on the published `3cf055d` and ABSENT at HEAD**, with `deny Exec`, `deny Exec f` and `pure f` all **1 -> 0** (`deny Unknown` correctly 0 both ways). Reviewer pinned the mechanism rather than reasoning it: `CANDOR_ALIAS_DEBUG=1` prints `BODYSHADOW f :: Cmd` on the fixture and nothing on the one-variable rename control; adding a single outer mention makes the counts differ, blocks the promotion and restores `['Exec']`. **Sixteen of seventeen positions are fixed; this is the seventeenth.** | Not fixed. **§E2, and the assertion is load-bearing:** `count_ident`'s own doc (`decls.rs:419-424`) asserts *"UNDERCOUNTING IS THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION … every position an identifier can occupy has to be reached."* **That sentence is false, and it was written by the commit that needed it to be true.** **Why no instrument caught it, which is the reusable part:** the 334+77 suite is green with the defect present, and **R119's corpus A/B could not have seen it** — this shape produces no `BODYSCOPE-ADD/DROP` because it is identical under the flattened and promoted collectors, so it sat inside the `CHANGED 0` the A/B read as quiet. A zero-diff over a shape the counters cannot distinguish is not evidence. **Prevalence: 0 instances in the 1490-crate registry**, so it is low-frequency — and it is still a regression against the published binary, in the sin direction, in the release candidate. **CLOSED — AND MY STATED MECHANISM WAS WRONG, in a way that decided the fix.** I briefed *"`count_ident` counts macro INVOCATION token streams but never a local `macro_rules!` DEFINITION body"*. **A `macro_rules!` written INSIDE the function body IS reached** — `Stmt::Item(Item::Macro)` -> `visit_macro` -> `in_tokens`, measured, shadow correctly dropped at both PRE and FIX. The real mechanism is that the definition `local_macros` re-injects sits at **FILE level, frequently in ANOTHER FILE**, so it is not in `block` at all and **no deeper AST descent could ever have reached it**. That is why the fix consults `local_macros` instead of widening the walk — a fix built on my wording would have walked harder and found nothing. Sixth brief this session corrected by measurement. Fix at `decls.rs:466-560` / `collector.rs:3800`: `count_ident(block, name, local_macros)` expands a bare `NAME!` through **`collector::macro_template_blocks`, the collector's OWN helper**, made `pub(crate)` rather than reimplemented (§G). Where that helper is opaque (multi-arm, `$(..)*`) the raw arm tokens are counted — a deliberate over-count, decoupled so this count's soundness does not depend on a precision decision in another file. Counting is SYMMETRIC, so a macro invoked inside the declaring block is still promoted (`macro_rules!` is unhygienic for items and types). The recursion guard is push/pop, **not** a once-per-count seen set, and that distinction is now pinned by a test. **§9 — 18 positions audited, every fixture compiled and RUN, with ground truth taken from a marker file touched by a spawned script rather than the function's return value** (three arms return `true` for reasons unrelated to spawning — an instrument choice that prevented three false passes). **Six were silent at PRE and are correct now**: a file-level macro, a macro in ANOTHER FILE, a template invoking a second macro, an invocation nested in another macro's opaque arguments, a REDEFINED macro name, and a self-mentioning template. Already reached: plain expressions, invocation tokens, attribute token lists, `cfg_if!` arms, `#[cfg]`-gated code. Not reached and nothing injects them: doc comments, string literals, `include!` text. **THE INSTRUMENT POINT WAS ADDRESSED, AND IT CHANGED THE READ OF THE A/B.** New `BODYMACRO-REACH`/`BODYMACRO` counters, proven output-inert over 62 crates, exist because **R119's `BODYSCOPE-ADD/DROP` compare against the flattened collector, which gives this shape the SAME answer** — R119's A/B could not have emitted anything about it. Corpus A/B, 1490 crates, wide key: 252,772 common rows, **ADDED 0 REMOVED 0 CHANGED 0**, 1490/1490 exit 0, zero zero-byte results. **The branch WAS reached — 6 promotions flip to DROP in portable-atomic — and the agent traced why none moves a row rather than assuming: `cmpxchg16b` is not a key in that file's `use` map, so the pre-fix shadow was already a no-op.** It therefore reports the A/B as an **over-charge control only**, with the executed fixture as the sole recall evidence. That is §E1 applied against the agent's own result. Revert test done BY REVERTING: `r139_a_local_macro_template_counts_toward_the_body_shadow` fails (`left: []`, `right: ['Exec']`). Each test was separately proven able to fail, and the commit records that only the `invoked twice` arm catches a seen-set recursion guard while the monotonicity test does not — stated rather than glossed. **§E2:** `decls.rs:419-424`'s false *"every position an identifier can occupy has to be reached"* is replaced with a checkable property — *every ident the `CallCollector` resolves through the `uses` map while walking this body* — bounded by the fact that `local_macros` is the only `CallCollector` field carrying token-level syntax from outside the body. **[Label corrected 2026-09-03 by the 0.35.0 release panel: `3cf055d` is NOT the published 0.34.0 — the v0.34.0 tag is `736fa64`, six commits earlier; where this row says 'published 3cf055d' read 'post-tag 3cf055d'. | R148 java: `soundness/dynamic/corpus.sh` prints ERROR and then `RESULT: CLEAN`, exit 0 | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-java `08293b5`, per Tom's 2026-09-03 ruling. Coordinator-verified: the real script in its real location exits 3** | **§H aggregator failure, java.** The harness prints `async-netfs: ERROR (classpath missing)` and then `RESULT: CLEAN` with exit 0 — a driver that never ran reaching the verdict as a pass. Cause is environmental: `AsyncNetFs.java` uses `Thread.ofVirtual()` (Java 21+) on a JDK 17 box, so it never compiles. But the aggregator cannot tell 'did not run' from 'found nothing', which is this project's standing instrument class — the same shape as R104's oracle and R108's eval gates. | Not fixed, and the agent's reason for leaving it is sound: **changing an aggregator's verdict is a separate decision with its own CI risk**, and it should not ride along inside a fabrication fix. The fix is the one R104 already established for the sibling instrument: **an ERROR'd driver must be UNRUN, not CLEAN, and must not exit 0.** Worth doing together with a check that the JDK actually supports every driver it is about to run. **MECHANISM LINE-PINNED by the R163 agent (still not fixed, deliberately):** `compile_all` (`soundness/dynamic/corpus.sh:51-61`) never reads `javac`'s exit code and prints `== compiled ==` regardless; the `async-netfs` entry then hits `ERROR (classpath missing: …)` at `:136` and **`return`s without touching `TOTAL_UNDERREPORTS`**, so one of seven dynamic-oracle legs silently never runs and the gate prints `RESULT: CLEAN`, exit 0. Cause on this machine: `javac` on PATH is 17 while the Gradle toolchain is 21, so `Thread.ofVirtual()` does not compile. Reproduces identically before and after R163. Detection worked; aggregation discarded it. The decision it needs — hard-fail on a missing toolchain, or SELFSKIP loudly — is Tom's, which is why two agents in a row left it alone. **RULED by Tom 2026-09-03: a missing toolchain makes `soundness/dynamic/corpus.sh` SELFSKIP loudly (exit 3, the family convention), never CLEAN and never FAIL. Change to land after the 0.35.0 cut.** **FIXED.** Mechanism: `compile_all` ran seven bare `javac` calls without reading an exit code, so a compile failure left a classes dir silently absent (here `AsyncNetFs.java:29`'s `Thread.ofVirtual()` against a JDK 17 `javac`); `entry()`'s classpath-missing branch then appended an ERROR summary line and RETURNED without touching `TOTAL_UNDERREPORTS`, so that leg evaporated and every other entry being fine still reached `RESULT: CLEAN`, exit 0. Now `compile_one` captures each rc and its first error line, the missing entry is recorded, and `main` checks a genuine finding FIRST (always exit 1) before reporting SELFSKIP. **All three arms executed, not reasoned about:** missing prerequisite -> exit 3 with `RESULT: SELFSKIP - 1 corpus entry could not be analysed (prerequisite missing): async-netfs (javac exit 1 ...)`; the same corpus with that entry excluded -> exit 0 CLEAN; a real finding (a test hook stripping `Fs` from `hof-io`'s report, caught by the real JFR oracle) -> exit 1, NOT 3. Coordinator re-ran the committed script: rc=3, and the final line names the entry and the reason. **Stated, not hidden:** `bin/gate-run.sh` never invokes this script - `bin/gates.sh candor-java` lists it as a `~` BLOCK line inside a conditional CI multi-step, one of the ~91 lines the harness defers to manual reading, so the SELFSKIP classification is unproven in situ; the mechanism it relies on is a bare rc==3 check at `gate-run.sh:197` with no repo-specific logic, which arm (a) satisfies. Wiring the script into an auto-run gate is a separate change, in a repo that agent did not own. |

Found by a whole-project review, not by any diff-scoped reviewer** | **THE FOUR-WAY SUITE CAN SILENTLY MEASURE A STALE ENGINE.** `conformance/run.sh:377` runs `( cd "$SW_DIR" && swift build >/dev/null 2>&1 )` in a subshell and **never reads its status**, then proceeds on `[ -x "$SW_BIN" ]` — the mere EXISTENCE of `.build/debug/candor-swift`. So a failed build falls through to whatever binary a previous run left behind, and the suite reports a four-way result for a swift that is not the tree. **This is the instrument I relied on all day**, running it four times and reporting OK each time. | Not fixed — deliberately held while five reviewers were reading these trees, and it must be fixed before the next run is trusted. **Coordinator-verified that it did NOT bite today, behaviourally rather than by mtime:** the debug binary carries both R130 fixes (`native:symlink` present; `URL.checkResourceIsReachable -> ['Fs']`), so today's four runs measured a current engine. **That is luck, not design** — the builds happened to succeed. The fix is to capture the status on its own line and treat a failed build as a hard FAIL, never as a skip and never as a pass — the same ruling R104 already made for the syscall oracle, which printed `build failed — SKIP`, summarised `0 NEW failure(s)` and exited 0. **The suite that catches this class in engines has it in itself.** **FIXED before the next run that would have relied on it.** The status is now captured on its own line and a present-but-unbuildable swift is a **HARD FAIL, exit 2** — never a skip, never a pass. `swift` on PATH plus a `Package.swift` means the engine is DECLARED, and *declared but broken* is exactly the case a green tick must not cover. Guard proven to FIRE, not assumed: simulated against a non-existent tree, `SW_BUILD_RC=1` and the exit-2 path is taken. **And I introduced a bug writing the fix, which is worth recording because it is the same class:** the error message contained `` `swift build` `` inside double quotes — **command substitution, so printing the failure would have RUN a build**. `bash -n` passes it because it is syntactically valid. Caught by grepping the new block for backticks in executable lines. Verified my edit adds no shellcheck findings: 107 exist in this file and rc=1 both before and after the change (`git stash` A/B), and none is in the new block; the file is in no shellcheck gate. **C3 rider from the same review, NOT fixed here and filed as remaining work:** roughly ten further swift blocks (from `:9777`, including PART 80's own `else` at `:15116` which falsely prints *"no swift toolchain"*, and PART 83 at `:15841`) still gate on `-x "$SW_BIN"`. They are bounded — a present-but-unbuildable swift now dies at this guard before reaching them — so they are reachable only through `part.sh` filtered slices, which is where the mislabels remain. |

The cheaper discriminator (a digest carried in the dep report) was REJECTED as producer-controlled — a hand-edited report keeps it, fail-open on a legitimate input. **Side finding:** the digest's identity-hash guard was quadratic and untested — 1.19 s of a 1.46 s digest on 15.4 MB; anchored and possessive it is 0.05 s, with a linearity test that fails at 1,262 ms against the old pattern. **Corpus A/B keyed on FIFTEEN fields, §E1 counters: 45,150 executions of the changed branch.** SAFETY: unchanged dep report → `+0/-0/~0` on all six consumers, reuse identical. A third artefact: dropping the `.hierarchy.json` sidecar beside a perturbed report also invalidated pre-fix for the wrong reason. Each is the *fixture reached the code?* question, answered by the agent on itself. Teeth, all revert-tested BY REVERTING: `RefreshDepDigestTest` (end-to-end arm plus a REFLECTIVE arm requiring every `DepFn` field to move the digest — reverted, it names all ten); **`bin/refresh-equiv.sh` gains a `CANDOR_DEPS` axis** that compiles its own two-package fixture, generates both dep reports with the jar under test, refuses to pass unless the perturbation moved a cold report AND the cache engaged, and **FAILS against the pre-fix jar**; `RefreshIdentityHashGuardTest` 7 positives / 7 near-misses / linearity. `ci/self-gate.sh` and `soundness/dynamic/corpus.sh` run by hand, both clean; `nativeCompile` UNRUN and identical at the merge-base. **INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION, against the artifact rather than the code.** A second agent, told not to read the fixer's fixtures, downloaded the published jar with `gh release download v0.34.0` (sha256 `ef77745a…b044`, self-reporting `candor-java 0.34.0` at spec ⟨0.34⟩, build stamp `c1486dd`) and used the fixed jar (sha256 `fb52aa23…96d5`, stamp `00b4a9f`). Its own fixture: `chained.Client.call()` v1 reads a file, v2 also opens a socket to a localhost listener — both EXECUTED (`NO CONNECTION` / `CONNECTION RECEIVED: b'*'`); `myapp.App` compiled once against each dep, **`App.class` sha256-identical either way**; dep reports produced by SCANNING each dep version with the jar under test, not hand-written; both jars agree the key `chained/Client.call()V` is stable while the value moves `['Fs']` → `['Fs','Net']` — the exact shape. Matrix, statuses on their own lines: cold v1 → 0 both jars; cold v2 → 1 both; **warm primed under v1, rerun under v2 — published: exit 0, `refresh — reused 1 of 1`, `App.main` `['Fs']` with `Net` silently missing; fixed: exit 1, `reused 0 of 1`, `['Fs','Net']` + `incomplete:['Net']`**; control primed v2 / rerun v2 → exit 1 with `reused 1 of 1` on both, so the fix did not break ordinary reuse. The cache engaged in the defect arm on both jars (non-zero reuse line), so the arm is not vacuous. **Two interface facts the verifier had to derive from source, reported as findings:** `CANDOR_REFRESH` (and `CANDOR_REFRESH_DEBUG`/`_VERIFY`) appear in neither `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, nor `--help` — zero hits in all three — and reuse is disclosed only on stderr, never in the report envelope. See R164. **Coordinator note:** the verifier's `mkdir` landed in a scratch directory the fixer had already populated under the same generic name; it abandoned that directory unread and worked in a uniquely-named one. The scratchpad is a shared instrument (CLAUDE.md) and two agents on one row will collide on a natural name — worth naming the directory in the brief. |

| R154 ts: a policy ALIAS line disarms the ⟨0.30⟩ fail-closed INCOMPLETE verdict | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-ts `c8e52bc`. Here an alias line deletes the disclosure. | Not fixed. Filed with two siblings from the same pass, both also executed and both positive-purity-by-absence: **a class EXPRESSION is invisible to CHA** (`const SubExpr = class extends Base {}` — `viaBase` vanishes; `pure a.viaBase` goes exit 1 -> exit 0 *"policy ✓"*), and a **named** class expression drops the interface-union entry entirely where the anonymous form correctly hedges `['Unknown']` and the declaration form gives `['Fs']`. Three spellings of one construct, three different answers — §F1 q7 at the language level rather than the table level. **RE-MEASURED 2026-09-04 and NEWLY REPRODUCED — the row previously recorded that the coordinator had NOT reproduced it.** Built the ⟨0.30⟩ `outOfScope` shape independently: an excluded file performing `Fs`. `deny Fs` alone → exit 2, `outOfScope` populated, `excluded[].peeked:true`. Adding `deny Unknown[corp]` beside it (with `.candor/config: unknown-alias corp = reflect`) → exit 0 'policy ✓', and **`outOfScope` is ABSENT ENTIRELY while `excluded[].peeked` flips to FALSE — the peek is never attempted, not merely emptied**. Silent once triggered: no `incomplete`, no disclosure at all. Fixture `scratchpad/stale-engines/``ts/r154/pkg2`. **FIXED, and the row was NARROWER than the defect.** Mechanism, proven directly against the published `policy.mjs` before anything was written: `scan.mjs:8751` called `parsePolicy(readFileSync(policyPath), {})` while the §6.2 gate 800 lines below passed the real `.candor/config` alias map. **`{}` is not an empty alias map — it has no `.has`**, and `policy.mjs:458` reads `else if (aliases && aliases.has(cn))`: truthy, then TypeError. That throw landed in the peek's bare `catch`, `peekPolicy` stayed null, `peekAttempted` was never set — so `outOfScope`/`scannedUnder` were ABSENT rather than empty, `peeked` false, and ⟨0.30⟩'s INCOMPLETE verdict never armed. **Three ways the row understated it:** the trigger is ANY `Unknown[<token>]` for which the map is needed, not a defined alias — `Unknown[nosuchalias]` disarms the peek identically and is only non-silent because the gate's own §6.2 refusal happens to hold the exit at 2; a second silent instance the row does not have is a `.candor/config` RESERVED-NAME line (`unknown-alias reflect = native`) beside a used alias, exit 0 → 2; and there is a second symptom independent of the verdict — the ⟨0.33⟩ ASKED-AND-CLEAR answer was deleted too, so `deny Unknown[corp]` over a tree with nothing excluded emitted NEITHER key, the document a no-policy scan produces. `Unknown[reflect]` never reaches the alias arm, and that reflect/corp pair is what isolates it. **Fix:** the vocabulary is parsed once, anchored at the policy file (the anchor the gate already used), memoized, and read by both paths; the peek's `try` now covers the READ only, and alias-DEFINITION errors fold into `fatalPolicyErrors` — **guard-deletion measured**: without the fold, a malformed alias makes the peek publish `outOfScope:['runs']` and `peeked:true` BESIDE `policy NOT evaluated` in the same run. One behaviour change rides along and is PINNED rather than asserted away: `discoverConfigText` exits 2 on an unreadable config and now runs at the peek, so for a policy filed in its own tree the refusal lands BEFORE the envelope is written — report WRITTEN at 0.35.0, NONE now, the §3.1 posture. Suite 2202/0; revert → 4 rows red (two are green on revert BY CONSTRUCTION because the pre-fix peek threw before reaching them, so the fold is pinned by the guard-deletion probe instead, and the commit says so); PART 48 and PART 53 ts arms driven directly on both builds — identical MATCH/OK, nothing pinned has moved. Arms verified by sha256 against a freshly packed 0.35.0 tarball. |

The docs should change, and the durable fix is a conformance-side check of each `AGENTS.md` and each embedded contract **against the SPEC vocabulary** — otherwise the next tier-1 additive rung repeats this exactly. **THE DURABLE FIX IS IN:** `scripts/check_agents_vocabulary.py` derives the vocabulary MECHANICALLY from SPEC.md §1's effect table (11 declarable names; `Unknown` excluded because the SPEC defines it as a visibility marker, not a declarable effect) and checks eight doc copies plus four embedded `--agents` contracts (UNRUN, never green, when a binary is absent). Wired into `conformance.yml`'s `agents-doc-drift` job — **which previously checked out candor-spec alone and would have checked nothing but its own file**; the job now checks out all five siblings (source only), so the gate enforces. §E3: run BEFORE any fix it was red on all 8 docs and all 4 contracts for `Llm` — a gate proven able to fail before it was trusted. **AND THE PREMISE IN MY BRIEF WAS WRONG:** I expected it to stay red only on rust and java. Completion of ts/agents/swift is dispatched; rust/java are one-line doc edits handed to the agents that own those trees. **A PUSH-ORDER CONSTRAINT this creates, same class as the `c53baa2` one:** the CI job reads each sibling's `origin/main`, so **every sibling's doc fix must be pushed BEFORE candor-spec's workflow**, or the gate goes red on main for a defect that is already fixed locally. **COMPLETED across the family, with one self-inflicted red on the way.** ts and agents gained the four missing effects, swift gained the full enumeration and a REGENERATED embedded contract (`gen-agents-doc.py` + rebuild, verified live), java gained its first-ever vocabulary line with the embedded resource re-synced (`--agents \| grep -c Llm` 0 → 1; the gate now exits 0 for java). **candor-agents' CI went RED on the doc push:** it embeds `AGENTS.md` through `gen-agentsmd.py` exactly as swift does, the agent regenerated swift's copy but not agents', and agents' own embedded-contract drift gate caught the doc edit without its regen — **the doc-pinned-to-embedded mechanism this row is about, working as designed against the fix for this row.** Regenerated, gate green locally, pushed. rust's one-line edit is queued with the R160 agent; when it lands, every copy is pinned to SPEC §1 by the new gate and the candor-spec workflow can be pushed. |

Coordinator-diagnosed from CI's own log; local reproduction FAILED and that is stated** | **A weekly drift check that has never once run.** The job exists to re-fetch ~74 calibrated crates from live crates.io and diff the coverage manifest — the staleness answer the fast gate structurally cannot give. `gh run list` shows exactly TWO runs ever: today's first scheduled run at `3cf055d` and my `workflow_dispatch` at `c4571a9`, **both red at the same step, before any fetch or diff.** **Cause, from the runner's own log:** `info: note that the toolchain 'nightly-2026-06-14-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu' is currently in use (overridden by …/rust-toolchain)`. The step is named *"Build the stable scanner"* and runs bare `cargo build --release -p candor-scan`; `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` installs stable, but the repo's `rust-toolchain` file overrides any bare `cargo`, so it builds with the pinned NIGHTLY. `.cargo/config.toml` then forces `rustflags = ["-C", "linker=dylint-link"]` for every target, the job never installs `dylint-link`, and every dependency's build script dies at link: `error: linker \`dylint-link\` not found`. **The green sibling in `ci.yml:344` already documents both traps and escapes both** — `RUSTFLAGS= cargo +stable test …`, with a comment explaining that set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS` overrides the workspace linker and that `+stable` is deliberate. The failing workflow was written 17 days later without either. §F1 q3: one question — *how do we build on stable in CI* — answered twice, and the second copy never checked against the first. | Not fixed — the fix is one line (`coverage-gate-refresh.yml:37` → `RUSTFLAGS= cargo +stable build --release -p candor-scan`, matching `ci.yml:344`) and is held only because another agent owns candor-rust at the time of writing. Evidence that the fix works on the runner is the sibling job, green on every push. **Reproduction limit, stated rather than hidden:** I could NOT reproduce the failure on macOS. With `dylint-link` removed from PATH and an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, both `cargo +stable` and `cargo +nightly-2026-06-14` passed `-C linker=dylint-link` to rustc (44 invocations, verified with `-v`) and **succeeded anyway; a fake `dylint-link` placed as the only one on PATH was invoked 0 times, even by a direct `rustc -C linker=dylint-link main.rs`.** I do not know why Apple-target rustc accepts and ignores that flag here, and I am not guessing. The diagnosis rests on the Linux log, not on a local repro. **Two instrument lessons worth more than the fix:** (1) my first three reproduction arms returned **exit 127** — I had stripped `~/.cargo/bin` to hide the linker and hid `rustup` with it. A dead instrument, read as a result, twice in one day. (2) I had earlier reported *"no `rust-toolchain.toml`"* as evidence — the file is `rust-toolchain`, no extension; a `cat` of the wrong name is not a measurement of absence. **PROVEN ON THE RUNNER, THEN THE SAME DEFECT ONE LAYER DOWN.** With the one-line fix (`4ba1350`) the *"Build the stable scanner"* step went **green for the first time in the job's existence**, and so did the crates.io fetch — the diagnosis that would not reproduce on macOS was correct on Linux. The failure then moved to *"Regenerate covered.tsv / open.tsv"*: `generate.py` shells out to a **bare `cargo run`** for its `classify_check` helper, and that subprocess inherited the nightly pin and the dylint linker exactly as the step's own cargo had — `linker \`dylint-link\` not found` again, **after 74 crates were fetched and 21,999 candidate entries collected.** The diff and issue steps were correctly skipped, so this was still not drift being reported. A per-step `+stable` cannot reach a cargo that Python spawns, so the second fix (`candor-rust`, follow-up commit) sets the decision ONCE at the job level: `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN: stable` and a set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS`. **Both halves measured, not assumed:** bare `cargo --version` inside the repo prints `1.98.0-nightly` and with the variable prints `1.98.0` stable — so the env var beats the toolchain file; and set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS` overriding the workspace linker is the fact `ci.yml:344` already relies on. **And forcing stable is the RIGHT mechanism rather than a workaround**, checked before choosing it over the alternative of installing `dylint-link` the way `ci.yml`'s ubuntu lane does: `classify_check` depends only on `candor-classify` and serde — no `#![feature]`, no `rustc_private`, no dylint — so it is a stable crate that was being built on nightly for no reason. Installing the linker would have satisfied the error while leaving the wrong toolchain in place. Re-dispatched; **the job's first-ever complete green, or its first-ever real drift finding, is the outcome to check next** — either would be a first. **CLOSED — verified 2026-09-07 by RUNNING the gate, which is the only thing that could close it.** The fix had ALREADY landed in `2d3d9a0` (2026-09-04) and was never exercised: the workflow is weekly, its only failing run sits at `f991ff1` (2026-09-03), and `2d3d9a0` is NOT an ancestor of it — so the red on the board was a report about code that no longer existed, and the row stayed OPEN for three days for that reason alone. A `workflow_dispatch` on `main` (`34140458495`) now gets PAST the step every previous run died at: **step 4 'Build the stable scanner' succeeds**, as do the generator selftest, `classify_check`, the 74-crate live fetch and the regeneration. `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN: stable` + set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS` at JOB level (not a per-step `+stable`, which cannot reach a cargo that Python spawns) is the mechanism. **The job still exits 1, but at step 9 — the drift diff — which is the gate DOING ITS JOB and its first real verdict ever: 0 regressed (no `classify()` rule lost), 2 oracle-dropped, 128 newly-uncovered.** Those 128 are NOT a finding yet: the gate's own output invokes R212 — the checked-in manifest is historically macOS and this run is the Linux runner, a same-commit run has measured a different row count across the two, and the host-platform cause was never ruled out. **A gate that has never run is not a gate, and a fix nobody exercised is not a fix** — the same shape as the `net_dns` mutation anchor and `gate-run.sh`'s unrun `self-gate`, all three found on one day. |

Coordinator-verified pre- and post-fix on the fixture and on rusqlite 0.40.2** | **CARDINAL SIN, rust, and it is rusqlite's front door.** `impl Conn { pub fn open(p) { Self::open_with_flags(p, 0) } pub fn open_with_flags(p, f) { std::fs::read_to_string(p)… } }` — `open_with_flags` reports `['Fs']`; **`open` is ABSENT** from `functions[]` on the manifest-era scanner (`79546f3`), the published `3cf055d`, and HEAD. **The control differs in exactly one token:** `open2` written as `Conn::open_with_flags(p, 0)` instead of `Self::…` reports `['Fs']`. So a `Self::`-qualified static call to a sibling is not resolved as a call edge, the forwarder reaches nothing, and the *mint no unit unless it carries or reaches an effect* filter omits it. Real instance: **`rusqlite::Connection::open`** (and `open_in_memory`) — absent on rusqlite 0.40.2 under all three scanners while `Connection::open_with_flags` reads `['Db','Unknown']`. The 08-28 coverage manifest still records `rusqlite::Connection::open` as covered, so an even earlier scanner reported it; when it was lost is not yet bisected. | Not fixed. **Gate consequence is the R79/R85 shape**: the callee is reported so a blanket `deny Db` fires incidentally, but `deny Db <caller>`, `pure <caller>`, `path`, `callers` and every question about WHO reaches the effect are blind for the function users actually call. **Fix direction:** resolve `Self::name(...)` in a static-call position exactly as `<EnclosingType>::name(...)` — one authority, not a second arm — and sweep `Self::` in every position the existing type-qualified resolution handles (assoc fn, assoc const, trait static, `<Self as Trait>::`). **FIXED, and the row's one dating claim corrected.** I wrote that an earlier scanner must have reported `rusqlite::Connection::open` because the 08-28 manifest lists it covered. **Wrong:** the oldest buildable scanner (0.3.5, `2952f78`, 2026-06-11) is already ABSENT on the fixture and on rusqlite, and `git log -S` over the whole history finds no commit that ever contained a `Self`-headed resolution arm. **`Self::` resolution never existed**; the manifest row is R162's stale-manifest story, not a scanner regression. **Fix (§G, one authority):** `scan_items` binds `Self` in the ordinary `use` map for the length of one impl block (and to the trait inside a trait default body, as that arm already does for `self`); `lang::expand` — the single place a written path becomes a resolved one — then answers every position, so there is no second arm. The value is `expand(<type>, uses)`, not the bare leaf, so `use inner::Far; impl Reach for Far` resolves `Self::touch` to `inner::Far::touch` exactly as `Far::touch()` does. **Position sweep, built and run:** assoc fn, `map(Self::f)`, nested closure, 3-deep chain, trait-default `Self::helper()`, `Self::new()` in a generic impl, impl-for-imported-type — all fixed. `Self::CONST` and `Self::Variant(..)` expand but match no fn unit, so nothing is charged (parity with `K::NAME`/`State::Open(n)`); **a leaf fallback was deliberately NOT used** because it would have linked `Self::NAME` to an effectful free fn `NAME` beside it. **Still under-reporting, stated:** `<Self as Trait>::m` with no default body (its `<Type as Trait>::m` control fails identically, so not a `Self` issue), `Self::required()` in a trait default, `impl Trait for &[u8]`. **Corpus A/B, 1489 crates, HEAD vs HEAD+fix, every field:** ADDED 2621, **REMOVED 2**, CHANGED 9227 — `calls` +7982, `invisible` +2969, `direct` −2094, `unknownWhy` −2094, **`inferred` +929 pure gain, 0 loss, 0 mixed**, `unresolved` +736, `incomplete` +88. §E1: `SELFALIAS` hit **231,409 times across 1037 of 1490 crates, 0 in the PRE arm**. Both removals audited from source — `crossbeam_epoch::Owned::with_tag` at two versions, whose `self.into_usize()` calls `mem::forget(self)`, so no drop runs: POST correct, PRE was an over-charge. **Near-miss reported, not hidden:** that row is right for a shaky reason — the escape gate is LEAF-keyed and suppresses the drop charge for a consumed by-value `self` whenever the function returns a fresh value of the same type leaf, forget or no forget; on a synthetic `via_self` its `G::mk()` control is absent in BOTH arms, so the fix brings `Self::` into parity with an existing silence rather than creating one (R168). The `unknownWhy` −2094 cluster is js-sys, where the reason moves one hop to the resolved callee; `deny Unknown[native]` over js-sys returns 180 violations in both arms. **Gate consequence:** blanket `deny Fs` fires in both arms via the callee; **`pure Conn::open` and `deny Fs Conn::open` go exit 0 → 1 on the lone-forwarder fixture `scratchpad/rsfwd-iso`** (PRE: the rule binds no unit; HEAD: 1 violation). Two coordinator instrument errors on the way, stated so they are not repeated: `pure rsfwd` is a crate-name scope that binds nothing and returned 0 on both arms; and on the multi-fn fixture `rsfwd` the scope `Conn::open` also binds `open2`/`open_with_flags`, so both arms exit 1 there and only the COUNT moves (2 → 3) — an exit-code control needs a scope that binds the forwarder alone. rusqlite 0.40.2: `Connection::open`/`open_in_memory` ABSENT → `['Db','Unknown']`, `functions[]` 276 → 283. 6 regression tests red on revert; the 7th is an absence control recorded as passing both ways. `gate-run.sh` 25/25, re-run by the coordinator after the agent finished: 25/25 again; `soundness/realworld/run.sh` under Docker with the fix in tree: 20 honest, 0 under-reports, 0 fabrications. **One anomaly the agent could not explain and reported:** a single `gate-run` pass of `21 ok, 4 failed` right after its R155 commit, with four subsequent 25/25 runs and the log directory already cleaned — re-run by the coordinator rather than accepted. **Both adjacent free-fn shapes measured against the fixed binary, and both are DIFFERENT mechanisms:** crossterm's `pub fn size() { sys::size() }` is still absent — `sys` is a `mod`, not a `use`, so `expand` leaves `sys::size` and its tail2 does not match the unit keyed `terminal::sys::unix::size` (module-path arm; R169); R165's bare `from_handle(path)` has no `::` at all, so `ctor_leaf_from_call_path`'s `rsplit_once` yields None — not covered. **[Label corrected 2026-09-03 by the 0.35.0 release panel: `3cf055d` is NOT the published 0.34.0 — the v0.34.0 tag is `736fa64`, six commits earlier; where this row says 'published 3cf055d' read 'post-tag 3cf055d'. The row's conclusion was re-checked against the true tag build where the panel could, and stands.]** |

Alias arm confirmed; the `Option<fn>` arm was MIS-STATED: `.unwrap()()` always disclosed, the silence is in the UNWRAP BINDERS (if-let/match/.map) and only for a BARE fn pointer. Coordinator-verified on rusqlite 0.40.2** | **CARDINAL SIN, rust — an explicit EMPTY effect set is a positive purity claim.** On identical rusqlite 0.40.2 sources: manifest-era scanner `79546f3` → `['Db']` for both; published `3cf055d` → `[]` for both. Both functions hand a raw `sqlite3` handle to FFI (`sqlite3_auto_extension`; `from_handle` wraps `*mut sqlite3`). Bisect over the 16 scanner commits `79546f3..3cf055d` (`crates/candor-scan`, `crates/candor-classify`), each built and run on the same sources: #8 `e43eec0` `['Db']`, #10 `2b98e1e` `['Db']`, #12 `0576b8c` `[]`. **Remaining suspects: #11 `7af62f1` (*drop glue fired on the BINDER, so 16 of 17 executed positions were silent*) and #12 `0576b8c` (*drop glue: the escape gate suppressed on SOME-path escape*).** `Connection` carries an `impl Drop` that closes the handle, which is why the drop-glue commits are the plausible pair. **Caught only by the coverage-gate job that had never run** (R157): the fast gate checks `classify()` against the manifest and cannot see a scanner change. | Not fixed; one bisect step remains and then the mechanism. **Do not assume the drop-glue commits are wrong in themselves** — they closed sixteen silent positions; the question is whether a guard they added mis-fires on a `Drop`-carrying type whose method reaches FFI. **CULPRIT `7af62f1`, mechanism named with an executed fixture and a flip-check.** #10 `2b98e1e` `['Db']` → #11 `7af62f1` `[]` for both functions; #12 only tightens SOME-vs-EVERY-path escape for branching code and these have no branches. `7af62f1` added `escaping_ctor_leaves`: a construction of a `Drop`-carrying type whose bound name reaches the function's tail/return is treated as escaping and the drop is not charged to that frame — the fix for flate2's constructors (R49's class). In `from_handle`, `InnerConnection::new(db,false)` is bound by `let`, then `db` sits inside `RefCell::new(db)` in the struct literal that IS the tail — escaping. Fixture `scratchpad/dropglue-fixture` reproduces the flip on the same three binaries; **removing `impl Drop` makes the charge vanish on BOTH pre- and post-culprit**, so the charge was drop-glue attribution and nothing else. **THE PREMISE ATTACKED, AND HALF OF IT FELL.** Ground truth by execution (3/3 tests): `from_handle` returns WITHOUT dropping — the file still exists after the call — so **`[]` for `from_handle` is the correct per-frame answer, and the pre-culprit `['Db']` was exactly the over-charge `7af62f1` set out to remove.** That half of this row is retracted. **`init_auto_extension` is different:** its only route to `sqlite3_close` is the caller-supplied `ax: fn(Connection) -> Result<()>` — an opaque boundary — and the real scan reports a bare `[]` with **no `Unknown` at all**, while the fixture's analogue correctly keeps `Unknown`. Losing the disclosure along with the charge is the sin that stands; the likely cause — a plain `fn(T) -> U` (here via the type alias `AutoExtension`) not being classified as a callback boundary — is stated as a hypothesis, not a finding. **Two adjacent real gaps found while building the fixture** (filed as R165/R166). The mechanism deletes the graph edge to the `Drop` unit with no residual marker, which is what defeats scoped policies, `path`, `gains` and `fix-gate` while blanket `deny Db` still fires via the independently-listed `Type::drop` units. **Sound direction per the family's own rule:** carve out PROVEN-safe escapes only; an escape through a closure or through a free-fn constructor is not proven and should either charge or leave a tagged edge for the transitive walk to resolve. **THE OPEN HALF RESOLVED, and the row's framing fell with it.** Nine callback-parameter spellings, each an executing fixture (the callback really writes a file), scanned on the manifest-era, published and HEAD binaries: `fn(T)->U`, `F: Fn`, `impl Fn`, `&dyn Fn`, `Box<dyn Fn>`, a fn pointer in a struct field, and `unsafe extern "C" fn` all read `Unknown` + `callback:unresolved call` on all three — correct. **`f: Alias` where `pub type Alias = fn(T) -> U`, and `f: Option<fn(T) -> U>`, are ABSENT from `functions[]` on all three** — identical bytes pre- and post-`7af62f1`, and reproducible on a fixture with no `Drop` type at all, so orthogonal to the bisected commit. **Why `init_auto_extension` ever showed `['Db']`:** never from its own `ax: AutoExtension` callback — `is_callable_type` has never fired on that alias in any binary. It **inherited** `from_handle`'s over-charge transitively (`from_handle` is in its `calls` on all three). When `7af62f1` correctly removed that fabrication, the inherited charge vanished and exposed the pre-existing alias gap. **A correct verdict produced by an incorrect mechanism, unmasked by removing the over-charge — R147's shape exactly.** Two unrelated defects that flipped in one commit. **Mechanism, line-pinned:** `is_callable_type` (`crates/candor-scan/src/lang.rs:311-350`) matches `Type::BareFn` (:313), `Fn*` trait leaves (:318), and for a bare `Type::Path` only the wrappers `Box`/`Rc`/`Arc`/`Symbol` (:337-344); any other named path — an alias, or `Option<…>` — falls through to `false` (:339/:346-348). Every PARAMETER-position call site uses it raw: `seed_fn_typed_vars` (`lang.rs:362-375`), `collector.rs:2801` (closure param), `collector.rs:2980` (annotated `let`). **Only `record_return` (`decls.rs:1617-1639`) first calls `unwrap_result_option` (`lang.rs:734-745`)** — for a function's own RETURN type, never a parameter; that asymmetry is the `Option<fn>` arm. **And the alias table cannot help even if consulted:** the alias recorder (`decls.rs:1061-1073`) accepts only a `syn::Type::Path` RHS (:1065); `pub type AutoExtension = fn(Connection) -> Result<()>` has a `BareFn` RHS and is never recorded. **Same-mechanism proof in both directions:** on the real rusqlite source, one edit — `ax: AutoExtension` → `ax: fn(Connection) -> Result<()>` — turns `init_auto_extension` from `[]` into `['Unknown']` + `callback:unresolved call`; on the bisect fixture, the reverse edit (introduce the alias) makes the analogue **disappear from `functions[]` entirely** while its executed ground-truth test still passes. **Direction:** always silence. With other recorded calls present the loss surfaces as an explicit `[]` with no `unknownWhy`; when the callback is the function's ONLY call, the function is pruned from the report — an affirmative purity claim under SPEC §2 rule 3, the worse symptom. **Fix shape (not implemented; rust editor is busy):** (1) make `is_callable_type` alias-aware; (2) let the alias recorder capture a `BareFn` RHS (and any RHS carrying an `Fn*` leaf); (3) apply `unwrap_result_option` at every parameter-position call site as `record_return` already does. **Not established:** nested spellings (`&Option<fn>`, `Box<Alias>`, alias-to-`Box<dyn Fn>`, double aliases) and the three further `is_callable_type` sites at `collector.rs:1460/1491/1710`. **Instrument note from the agent, worth keeping:** the `HEAD` binary it used was built from a DIRTY working tree (the R160 agent mid-edit in `decls.rs`/`lang.rs`) — results were byte-identical to the two clean binaries for everything scanned, so its conclusions stand, but *`target/release/candor-scan` is not HEAD while an agent is editing that repo*. **FIXED.** `is_callable_type` gained a crate-wide `callable_aliases` index (collected in the same `syn::Item::Type` arm as `prim_aliases`) and now applies `unwrap_result_option`, which only `record_return` had ever done and only to a RETURN type; `elem_trait_leaves`' payload resolver gained a synthetic `"Fn"` leaf for a bare fn pointer — which is why `Option<Box<dyn Fn>>` always worked and `Option<fn>` never did. Executed 10-spelling fixture: `Alias`, `Box<dyn Fn>`-alias, `&Alias`, `Option<fn>` if-let and `.map` all ABSENT → `['Unknown']` + `callback:unresolved call`; purity controls unchanged; 2 tests red on revert. Coordinator-verified: `rusqlite::init_auto_extension` `[]` → `['Unknown']` on the real 0.40.2 source, `from_handle` stays `[]`. Cache rev17. Residuals, stated in code: a double alias and an alias-typed RETURN are still not resolved (Pass A builds the index and cannot consult it). **[Label corrected 2026-09-03 by the 0.35.0 release panel: `3cf055d` is NOT the published 0.34.0 — the v0.34.0 tag is `736fa64`, six commits earlier; where this row says 'published 3cf055d' read 'post-tag 3cf055d'. The row's conclusion was re-checked against the true tag build where the panel could, and stands.]** |
| R162 rust: the coverage generator drops `fs_err::file::open` from `covered.tsv` while every scanner reports it `['Fs']` | 2026-09-02 | **RESOLVED as a PROCESS GAP, not a live generator defect; two real generator bugs found beside it, OPEN. Sonnet-investigated, read-only** | **A row the 08-28 manifest lists as covered (`Fs`/`Fs`) vanishes from BOTH fresh files on CI and on a local run against the same fs-err 3.3.1 sources**, although the manifest-era, published and HEAD scanners all report `file::open` as `['Fs']` (exact-segment match). The generator is byte-identical to the one that produced the manifest. Investigation is read-only and scratch-only until the mechanism is known. **EXPLAINED — and the premise was wrong in the reassuring direction.** `fs_err::file::open` is `pub(crate) fn open` (`fs-err-3.3.1/src/file.rs:27`). `is_bare_pub` correctly excludes it, so it is **not a candidate today** and never reaches `classify_check`; `fs_err::create` (`file.rs:32`) is the twin. fs-err 3.3.1 is immutable and still crates.io's latest, so this is not a version change. **How the row got into the checked-in manifest:** `is_bare_pub` landed in `5a65219` on 2026-08-27, the day BEFORE the manifest was committed (`3a32fdf`, then `79546f3`) — yet the row survived all three commits untouched from the gate's first commit `2ab04a4`. (2) `root_reexports()` has been **dead code since the first commit** — computed and discarded (`# noqa: F841`) — so an intermediate-module alias like `terminal::supports_keyboard_enhancement` from `pub use sys::…` can never be a guess; **and that exact guess string is in the checked-in manifest and is unproducible by any version of the generator**, which is further evidence the manifest was not produced by this pipeline as-is. There is exactly ONE `try_exits.push` in the file (`lang.rs:3459`, inside `walk_expr`), and `walk_expr`/`walk_block` are called only on real `syn::Expr`/`syn::Block` nodes - the macro-token walks (`note_macro_ctor_leaves`, `note_macro_site`, `note_opaque_block`) are separate recursions that write only `interior`/`macro_ctor_leaves`/`opaque_interior_leaves`. **No `?` inside macro tokens can EVER become a `TryExit`.** A different mechanism wearing the same clothes, not one more case for R199. **NARROWER in one useful way:** silent only when the macro-borne `?` is the function's SOLE exit - a macro-`?` inside a `try_for_each` closure, or a second `?` written outside the macro, both rescue it. That is why corpus incidence was 0. Fix hazard is the `seq` number: a token-parsed expression has no position in the pre-order counter, and a `TryExit` with the wrong `seq` either vetoes nothing or vetoes every construction in the function. Monotone-safe placement is the enclosing `ExprMacro`'s own `seq` (can only make the `?` earlier, so it under-vetoes in the interior, never over-vetoes) and it must therefore be PAIRED with the `interior` term, not replace it. **Failing direction is under-veto: this fix can leave residual silence and must not be reported as closing the class.** **(b) invoked-closure tail - NOT the same subject, and it has no `?` in it.** Mechanism is `lang.rs:3527` `syn::Expr::Closure(c) => self.escapes.push(&c.body)`, an unconditional escape route re-united after the positional `?` filter. Invoked and NEVER-INVOKED are indistinguishable; arity-blind (one, two and three real drops all ABSENT); `let _ = f()`, a bare `f();`, binding without an explicit `drop`, invoking inside a block, and pushing the result into a locally-dying `Vec` are all silent; the only charging spelling is an explicit `drop(h)`, which is modelled independently of the closure. **This is the same line as R198's real cause - see that row; one line, 30 of 50 silent cells, currently recorded as two unrelated rows.** **PARTIALLY CLOSED — candor-rust `97c4e2a`, 2026-09-07.** The shared cause named above (`lang.rs`'s unconditional `escapes.push(&c.body)`) is now withdrawn in exactly one PROVABLE case: the let-bound closure's name is used, and EVERY use is the callee of a call whose value is discarded (`f();`, `let _ = f();`). **The LOOSE fix was built first and REJECTED** — withdrawing the suppression for every let-bound closure moved 18 cells and FOUR were FABRICATIONS against executed ground truth, from two distinct causes: a closure that is never invoked never runs its body (0 drops), and one whose result is returned hands the value to the caller (0 drops). The shipped rule moves **9 cells, all correct, zero fabrications**, including R209(b)'s own verbatim cell. **RESIDUAL, and this row is NOT closed:** five shapes the loose version also reached stay silent — the result bound and kept, invocation inside a block, a closure passed by reference — because their uses are not discarded-callee. Corpus A/B via `bin/corpus-ab.py`: 1,514 crates / 276,938 rows, ADDED 0 / REMOVED 0 / CHANGED 0 in all five key and value modes, which is evidence only because the branch is instrumented and REACHED **189 times across 90 real crates** — in every one the closure body held no drop-relevant construction. Same zero-incidence profile as R194/R199. The sharded-slab case the line was written for is untouched BY CONSTRUCTION: an inline closure still pushes unconditionally. Regression test falsified against the unfixed engine (stashing `lang.rs` alone panics it). |

Checked mechanically before deciding: of 3,603 ambiguity sites in the 59 crates it touches, **all 134 rows it would withdraw had `distinct = 1`**, while 2,237 `distinct = 2` and 824 `distinct = 3` sites are genuine and stay. **2026-09-05, decision taken after a Fable review + a candor-spec port: TAKE the `by_leaf` half.** The deciding measurement is that a genuinely-differing `#[cfg]` twin does NOT go quiet — it goes to the UNION of both arms (`Fs` + `Exec`, order-independently), so this is a hedge replaced by a COMPLETE answer, not a withdrawal; the R208 analogy was INVERTED (that one went concrete → hedge). | R226 conformance: `part.sh <id>` reports a FALSE RED when a part builds its fixtures ABOVE its own `[id]` header — PART 4n today, PART 10 until 2026-09-05 | 2026-09-05 | **CLOSED 2026-09-06 — candor-spec `939a8c0` (PART 10) + this range (PART 4n). The CLASS is not closed: see the last cell** | **INSTRUMENT DEFECT, candor-spec, and it fails in the direction that LOOKS LIKE AN ENGINE BUG.** `part.sh` slices `run.sh` at the printed `[id]` markers, so a part whose predecessor ends on a HEADER rather than a `PART n` verdict has `end = limit` and the predecessor's slice swallows the next part's fixture setup. `part.sh 10` therefore built no `vocab.json` and fired its own 'the purpose-built ambiguity fixture produced NO `ambiguous:` reason' DIVERGE against a HEALTHY shipped `cc05b8c`; `part.sh 7 10` on the identical tree was green, which is the only reason the two reds were told apart. **PART 4n has the identical shape and is still live:** its `ext/report*.json` are written inside the previous slice, and `part.sh 4n` alone reports FAIL rust / FAIL java / FAIL ts plus a DIVERGE. Swept mechanically over all 88 slices: 4n is the only remaining false-red. 12b/12c under-reach the same way but die LOUDLY on an unbound variable (the documented safe shape); 4h, 4k, 16, 38, 84 read state an earlier part built and are the documented 'ask for both ids' case. **`part.sh`'s own header asserts the boundary rules 'can only UNDER-reach — a slice missing its own tail', which is FALSE in this direction:** an under-reach that loses a part's SETUP produces a hard DIVERGE, not a missing row. Invisible to CI (the full suite runs everything in order) and visible only to the person iterating on one part — exactly who trusts it. | Fixed by the cheap remedy: PART 4n's `mkdir -p "$W/ext"` and its three report heredocs now sit BELOW the `[4n]` header, with a comment saying why they must stay there. **Verified both directions on one tree: `part.sh 4n` alone was exit 1 with three FAILs and a DIVERGE before, exit 0 with MATCH after; the full `conformance/run.sh` is exit 0 / 80 parts both before and after, so the move changed no verdict.** `part.sh --check` still passes all 88 slices. **THE CLASS REMAINS OPEN AND THAT IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** Two instances have now been found the same way — by someone hitting a false red while iterating — and the guard that should find them cannot: `part.sh --check` verifies a slice PARSES and names exactly one part, never that it can still ANSWER, so it is blind to setup-annexation by construction. A third instance will be found the same expensive way. The durable remedy is a `--check` that RUNS each slice alone and compares its verdict to the same part's verdict in a full run; anything cheaper re-checks the property that already holds. Not built here — it is a new instrument, not a fix. |

| R230 rust: a by-value parameter of a Drop type is charged a `::drop` edge whether or not the callee drops it — a function that only STORES it is charged identically to one that drops it | 2026-09-06 | **OPEN — mechanism now MEASURED on an unambiguous fixture (0 drops anywhere). The test defect is fixed; the engine change is deliberately not made** | Each of the six asserted that a macro-borne construction on a `?` operand's by-value spine "stays exempt" from R172's site gate. R172 keys on sites and has **no by-value-argument exemption at all** — every one passed because the construction was invisible to the walk R229 fixed, and every one has a direct twin the gate has charged since published 0.34.0. They now assert parity with that twin. **The proposed follow-up — withdraw the caller-side `::drop` edge for a construction moved by value into a callee — is NOT supported yet, and the row is filed OPEN rather than actioned because the evidence offered for it cannot bear the claim.** The justification was "executed 0 in-frame drops", but the harness both the agent and I used forgets only the RETURNED value, so its counter runs for the whole dynamic extent of the call and counts the CALLEE's frame too. It cannot separate the two frames, which is the entire content of the claim. Measured on it, `byval_try` drops 1 — the opposite of the number quoted. **What I did establish, on four executed shapes:** where the callee is resolvable candor already models it dropping its by-value parameter (`use_h_val` carries `calls=['H::drop']`), so the caller's extra edge is redundant, not load-bearing; where the callee is a fn pointer or a closure parameter the caller carries no drop edge at all and discloses `Unknown` instead. Executed, **0 drops ANYWHERE** — no frame-attribution question arises, which is what makes this fixture decisive where the earlier one was not. Measured: `keep` is charged `calls: ['H::drop']` -> **Fs. FABRICATION.** Its twin `eat(h: H) { let _ = h; }`, which really does drop, is charged identically and correctly. **So the engine charges a `::drop` edge for ANY by-value parameter of a Drop type, whether or not the body drops it** — the callee cannot tell storing from dropping. `caller_store` is then charged twice over: its own caller-side edge AND `keep`. **This makes the originally proposed fix insufficient, not merely unevidenced.** Withdrawing the CALLER-side edge alone leaves `keep`'s fabrication standing and does not clear `caller_store`, which stays charged through `keep`. The subject is R200's — the callee — and the caller-side edge is the second-order part. Still not fixed here: it withdraws a charge, which is a report-not-decide item under this run's stop conditions. Direction is over-report, the safe one. |

The 1980/1985 mtimes are not an objection — an archive-extracted file is instantly older than three days. **Decision cost, measured: migration is FREE today.** `$TMPDIR/candor-corpus` does not currently exist, so nothing would be orphaned by defaulting to `$HOME/.candor/corpus`; the trade is that a home-dir corpus is never auto-reclaimed, making `disk-guard.sh` the only thing between it and a full disk. It cannot, and no fix could.** An object-literal accessor is never minted as a UNIT, so `recordAccessorHit` has nothing to edge into and can only disclose `Unknown` — which is exactly what `{...lit}`, `Object.assign({}, lit)` and `lit.token` have always answered over the same literal. The achievable close is silence -> `Unknown[reflect]`, and the agent asserted that as an IDENTITY with those three existing spellings rather than as two lists that happen to agree. **It caught this in calibration and said so: a `deny Fs` framing would have been an instrument that could not fail.** **Verified by me at `f2d30a8` -> HEAD: `Object.entries`, `Object.values` and `JSON.stringify` over a literal all ABSENT -> `['Unknown']`, while the CLASS-INSTANCE control stays ABSENT** (R115's answer is correct and is the over-charge control), as do a data-property literal and `Object.keys`. Degrading `classBodiedGetter` to `return false` reddens exactly those five named rows. **Corpus A/B ADDED 0 / REMOVED 0 / CHANGED 0, and that zero is labelled SAFETY-ONLY in the commit and CHANGELOG at the time of writing.** It instrumented WHY rather than leaving the zero bare: 342 callsites reached, **0** at the charge site, because across those sites the argument types carried **2,621 properties and not one declared a getter of any kind** — so R115's exclusion was doing no work; there was nothing to exclude. **Priced on a RECALL harness instead**, five real published literals vendored verbatim (axios's fetch feature-probe getter, mongoClient's five, zod, hono): 7 caller rows ABSENT -> disclosed, **16 accessor names disclosed, exactly the 16 real getters those literals declare**. **Residuals pinned as tests rather than described:** `JSON.stringify({a: lit})` / `[lit]` / `structuredClone({a: lit})` invoke one level down (executed 1 each) and still report nothing; `console.log("%j", o)` and `util.inspect(o,{getters:true})` invoke (executed 1 each) on an argument no arm reads; `Object.assign`'s SOURCE arm is still text-keyed and reachable by a project's own `const Object = {...}` (pre-existing, not inherited by the new arms). **And a coverage gap in R116/R247's OWN block:** degrading the receiver-declares-an-accessor test reddened only the new R251 row, because no R116/R247 fixture has a target declaring a DATA property but no setter — their controls use `{}` and `Record`, which have no properties at all. |

Named entries include `sea_orm::database::restricted_connection` and `sea_orm::database::transaction::*` families, `sea_orm::driver::rusqlite::*`, `sea_orm::rbac::context::*`, `sqlx_core::config::Config::try_from*` and `ureq::tls::rustls::RustlsConnector::connect`. Each newly-uncovered entry is an effectful surface the classifier has no rule for, so the direction of the risk is a SILENT UNDER-REPORT on any crate reaching it — `sea_orm`'s connection/transaction families and `ureq`'s TLS connector are exactly the shapes the cardinal sin lives in. Triage the 128 into (a) genuinely new upstream API needing a classifier rule, and (b) entries an existing rule should already have matched, which would be a rule defect rather than drift. Only after that should the manifest be refreshed, and the refresh commit should say which of the two each row was. **PREMISE CORRECTED 2026-09-07, same day, by the experiment this row should have run first.** This row said the 128 were "real upstream crates.io movement". **They are not — they are OUR OWN SCANNER seeing more.** The oracle IS `candor-scan` (`generate.py` hardcodes `SCAN_BIN = target/release/candor-scan`), so every fresh arm used a scanner carrying today's work while the checked-in manifest was regenerated on 2026-09-03 (`7ac50f2`), essentially the v0.35.0 scanner. Separating the two axes by swapping the v0.35.0 tag binary into `target/release` and regenerating against the SAME sources: **crates.io drift alone (checked-in vs tag-scanner on today's registry) = 1 newly-uncovered, 3 oracle-dropped, 0 regressed. Scanner improvement alone (tag-scanner vs HEAD-scanner, identical sources) = 140 newly-uncovered, 0 oracle-dropped, 0 regressed.** Row counts: checked-in 1,256/461, tag-scanner 1,253/447, HEAD-scanner 1,317/587. **So the growth is R257/R264/R270/R271/R272/R206/R209(b)/R303/R304 landing between 09-03 and today**, each of which taught the scanner to see a call it previously missed. **This makes the finding BETTER, not weaker:** the generator's own docstring says an unmatched path in a CALIBRATED crate is treated as a claim of reviewed purity with no disclosure, so each of these is a public entry point where the oracle now sees a concrete effect and `classify()` silently claims pure. **61 of the 128 carry a HARD effect** — 43 `sea_orm` (mostly `Db,Log,Unknown`), 6 `redis`, 3 `deadpool_postgres` (`prepare_typed_cached`), 2 `sqlx_core`, 2 `rusqlite`, 2 `ignore` (incl. `walk::DirEntry::metadata`, `Fs` — a long-established method, which is what first broke the drift hypothesis), 1 `ureq`, 1 `postgres` (`CancelToken::cancel_query`, `Net`), 1 `native_tls_crate`; the other 61 are soft-only (Log/Rand/Clock). **Consequence for the remedy:** the triage split this row asked for is not "new upstream API vs rule defect" — it is "which of these did our own improvements expose", and the manifest refresh belongs WITH the scanner work that exposed them rather than as a separate housekeeping commit. The hard-effect 61 are the priority; a `Db` claimed pure on `sea_orm` is the cardinal sin on a widely-used crate. **ACCOUNTED 2026-09-07 after the 12 rules landed: 128 -> 122, and the arithmetic is the fix working, not a blind spot.** I expected 12 and got 6, and the leading hypothesis (R312's identifier-vs-resolved split leaving my new rules dead in the gate) was WRONG. Measured with `--diff-manifests`: **all 12 targets left the newly-uncovered set**, and **6 genuinely arrived** — `lettre::…::Identity::from_pem`, `reqwest::tls::Identity::from_pkcs12_der` and `::from_pkcs8_pem`, `sqlx_core::net::tls::tls_native_tls::handshake`, `tungstenite::tls::encryption::native_tls::wrap_stream`, `ureq::tls::native_tls::NativeTlsConnector::connect`. Every one is a native-TLS consumer: **R312's gate widening propagated `Fs` through `native_tls` and thereby EXPOSED six real uncovered entry points that were previously invisible because the rule upstream of them was dead.** Net -6 exactly matches CI. **The general shape worth keeping: closing a silent under-report can RAISE the uncovered count, because a rule that starts firing makes its consumers' gaps visible for the first time.** A gate count that fails to fall is not evidence the fix failed. One residual: `native_tls_crate::imp::security_framework::Identity::from_pkcs12` is covered under one spelling and still open under the other — the R312 split, not yet fully closed. |
